Canto 5 — Mathurā-khaṇḍa

Chapter 1 - Advice to Kaṃsa

śrī-nārada uvāca
vasudeva-sutaṁ devaṁ
kaṁsa-cāṇūra-mardanam
devakī-paramānandaṁ
kṛṣṇaṁ vande jagad-gurum 5.1.1

Śrī Nārada said: “I offer my respectful obiesances to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of the universes, the crusher of Kaṁsa and Cāṇūra, the son of Vasudeva, and the bliss of Devakī.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
mathurāyāṁ kiṁ caritraṁ
kṛtavān bhagavān mune
kathaṁ jaghāna kaṁsākhyaṁ
etan me brūhi tattvataḥ 5.1.2

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “O sage, what pastimes did the Supreme Personality of Godhead enjoy in Mathurā? How did He kill Kaṁsa? Please tell me this in truth.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
athaikadāhaṁ mathurāṁ purīṁ parāṁ
vilokituṁ cāgatavān nṛpeśvara
kartuṁ paraṁ daitya-vadhodyamaṁ hareḥ
parasya sākṣān manasā praṇoditaḥ 5.1.3

Śrī Nārada said: “O king of kings, one day, inspired by Lord Kṛṣṇa in my heart, I went to Mathurā City both to see it and to arrange for Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pastime of killing many demons.”

siṁhāsane ca prahṛte purandarāt
sitātapatre cala-cāru-cāmare
sthitaṁ nṛpaṁ kaṁsam uraṅga-duḥsahaṁ
prāvocam evaṁ śṛṇu tat-prapūjitaḥ 5.1.4

“After he had worshiped me, and as, fanned with beautiful cāmaras, he sat on a white-parasol throne stolen from Indra, I spoke to Kaṁsa, who was poisonous like a snake. Please hear what I said.”

yasodāyāḥ sutā jātā
yā tvad-dhastād divaṁ gatā
devakyāṁ kṛṣṇa utpanno
rohiṇī-nandano balaḥ 5.1.5

“The girl who fled to heaven from your hand was actually the daughter of Yaśodā. Devakī actually had a son: Kṛṣṇa, and Rohiṇī also had a son: Balarāma.”

sva-mitre nanda-rāje ca
nyastau putrau bhavad-bhayāt
tavārī rāma-kṛṣṇau dvau
vasudevena daitya-rāṭ 5.1.6

“O king of the demons, afraid of what you might do to them, Vasudeva placed his sons, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, who are your enemies, under the care of his friend Nanda Mahārāja.”

pūtanādyā hy ariṣṭāntā
daityā ye tvad-balotkaṭāḥ
yābhyāṁ hatā vanoddeśe
te mṛtyū tau smṛtau kila 5.1.7

“In the forest these two boys killed the host of very powerful demons, beginning with Pūtanā and ending with Ariṣṭa, that you sent there. Those two boys are like death personified.”

evam ukto bhoja-patiḥ
krodhāc calita-vigrahaḥ
jagrāha niśitāṁ khaḍgaṁ
śauriṁ hantuṁ sabhā-tale 5.1.8

“When he heard these words, Kaṁsa trembled with anger. He grabbed a sharp sword to kill Vasudeva in the royal assembly.”

mayā nivāritaḥ so ’pi
vistṛtair nigaḍair dṛḍhaiḥ
baddhvā taṁ bhāryayā sārdhaṁ
kārāgāre rurodha ha 5.1.9

“When I forbade him to do that, with great chains he tightly bound Vasudeva and his wife, and locked them in a prison cell.”

ity uktvā taṁ mayi gate
keśinaṁ daitya-puṅgavam
rāma-kṛṣṇa-vadhārthāya
preṣayām āsa daitya-rāṭ 5.1.10

“I spoke to Kaṁsa and then I left. When I was gone Kaṁsa sent the great demon Kesī to kill Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.”

cāṇūrādīn samāhūya
mahā-mātraṁ dvipasya ca
kārya-bhāra-karāl lokān
prāhedaṁ bhoja-rāḍ balī 5.1.11

“Calling together his many servants, headed by Cāṇūra and his elephant-trainer, Kaṁsa spoke to them the following words.”

kaṁsa uvāca
he kūṭa he tośalaka
he cāṇūra mahā-bala
rāma-kṛṣṇau ca me mṛtyū
darśitau nāradena tu 5.1.12

Kaṁsa said: “O Kūṭa, O Tośala, O powerful Cāṇūra, Nārada Muni has reveled to me that Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma are destined to kill me.”

bhavadbhir iha samprāptau
hanyetāṁ malla-līlayā
malla-bhūmiṁ ca samyuktaṁ
kurutāśu śubhāvahām 5.1.13

“When They come here you should kill Them in the wrestling games. In this way you will bring auspiciousness to our wrestling arena.”

dvipaṁ kuvalayāpīḍaṁ
raṅga-dvāri madotkaṭam
prasthāpya tena hantavyau
mahā-mātra mahāhitau 5.1.14

“O elephant-trainer, placed the enraged elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa at the gateway and it will kill my two enemies.”

caturdaśyāṁ tu kartavyo
dhanur-yāgaḥ praśāntaye
amāvāsya-dine lokā
malla-yuddhaṁ bhaved iha 5.1.15

“O people, to ensure that there is peace in our land, on the fourteenth day of the moon we will celebrate a Dhanur-yajña ritual. Then, on the new-moon day we will celebrate a festival of wrestling matches.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā sva-janān kaṁso
’krūram āhūya sa-tvaram
rahasi prāha rājendra
mantraṁ mantri-jana-priyam 5.1.16

Śrī Nārada said: “O king of kings, after speaking these words to his people, Kaṁsa called for Akrūra and in private gave the following counsel, a counsel that 168pleased Akrūra, the wisest of counselors.”

kaṁsa uvāca
bho bho dāna-pate mantrin
chṛnu me paramaṁ vacaḥ
gaccha nanda-vrajaṁ prātaḥ
kuru kāryaṁ mahā-mate 5.1.17

Kaṁsa said: “O counselor, O best of the generous, please hear my noble words. In the morning you should go to Nanda’s Vraja. O noble-hearted one, then you should execute the mission I give to you.”

āsāte tatra me śatrū
vasudeva-sutau kila
darśitau nāradenāpi
deva-devarṣiṇa bhṛśām 5.1.18

“My two enemies, the sons of Vasudeva, have gone there. Devarṣi Nārada revealed that to me.”

sopāyanair gopa-gaṇair
nanda-rājādibhiḥ saha
mathurā-darśana-miṣād
rathenānaya māṁ ciram 5.1.19

“One pretext of bringing Them to see Mathurā City bring, accompanied by King Nanda and the gopas bearing gifts, the two of Them to me at once.”

dvipena vā mahā-mallair
ghātayiṣyāmi tau śiśū
tat-paścān nanda-rājaṁ ca
vasudeva-sahāyakam 5.1.20

“I will have the two boys killed, either by the elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa, or by the great wrestlers, and then I will kill Vasudeva’s friend Nanda Mahārāja.”

vṛṣabhānu-varaṁ paścān
nava-nandopanandakān
paścac chauriṁ haniṣyāmi
devakaṁ tat-sahāyakam 5.1.21

“Then I will kill King Vṛṣabhānu, and then the nine Nandas and Upanandas, and then Vasudeva, and then Vasudeva’s friend Devaka.”

ugrasenaṁ ca pitaraṁ
vṛddhaṁ rājya-samutsukam
tat-paścād yādavān sarvān
haniṣyāmi na saṁśayaḥ 5.1.22

“Then I will kill my old father, who still yearns to rule his kingdom, and then I will kill all the Yādavas. Of this there is no doubt.”

ete deva-gaṇāḥ sarve
jātā mantrin mahī-tale
śakunir me mahā-mitro
balī candravatī-patiḥ 5.1.23

“O counselor, all the demigods are now born on the earth. Still, Śakuni, the powerful king of Canrāvatī, is my great friend.”

bhūta-santāpano hṛṣṭo
vṛkaḥ śambara eva ca
kālanābho mahānābho
hariśmaśrus tathaiva ca 5.1.24

“Bhūtasantāpana, Hṛṣṭa, Vṛka, Śambara, Kālanābha, Mahānābha, and Hariśmaśru are also my friends.”

ete mitrāṇi me santi
mad-arthaṁ prāṇadā balāt
śvaśuro ’pi jarāsandho
dviviḍo me sakhā smṛtaḥ 5.1.25

“They are all my friends. They would give up their lives for my sake. Jarāsandha is my father-in-law. Dviviḍa is also my friend.”

bāṇāsuraś ca narako
mayy eva kṛta-sauhṛdaḥ
ete sarvāṁ mahīṁ jitvā
baddhvā devān sa-vāsavān 5.1.26

“Bāṇāsura and Narakāsura are also my friends. My friends will counquer all the worlds. They will put Indra and the demigods in chains.”

kṣiptvā meru-guha-durge
kuveraṁ dravya-nāyakam
trailokya-rājyaṁ tu sadā
kariṣyanti na saṁśayaḥ 5.1.27

“They will imprison the heavenly treasurer Kuvera in a cave on Mount Meru. They will rule the three worlds. Of this there is no doubt.”

kavīnāṁ tvaṁ kavir iva
girāṁ giṣpati-vad bhuvi
etat kāryaṁ ca kartavyaṁ
tvayā dāna-pate tvaram 5.1.28

“You are the wisest of wise men. In this world you are eloquent like Bṛhaspati. O generous one, please execute this mission at once.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
tvayā kṛto yadu-pate
manoratha-mahārṇavaḥ
daivecchayāyaṁ bhavati
goṣpadaṁ tad vinārṇavaḥ 5.1.29

Śrī Akrūra said: “O king of the Yādavas, you have a great ocean of desires. If destiny wishes, you will cross that ocean as one crosses a the puddle in a cow’s hoofprint. If destiny does not wish, your desires will remain an impassable ocean.”

kaṁsa uvāca
visṛjya daivaṁ kurute baliṣṭho
daivaṁ samāśritya hi nirbalaś ca
kālātmanor nitya-gayo prabhāvān
nirākulas tiṣṭhatu karma-yogī 5.1.30

Kaṁsa said: “A strong man rejects destiny. He makes his own destiny. Only the weak lean on destiny. Protected by the power of his own work, a strong man is never troubled by eternal time and the eternal Supersoul.”

Chapter 2 - The Killing of Keśī

śrī-nārada uvāca
ataḥ keśī mahā-daityo
haya-rūpi madotkaṭaḥ
etya vṛndāvanaṁ ramyaṁ
jagarja ghanavad balī 5.2.1

Śrī Nārada said: “Then the powerful and ferocious demon Keśī assumed the form of a wild horse. Neighing like thunder, he entered beautiful Vṛndāvana.”

yasya pāda-pratāḍena
nipetuḥ śākhino dṛḍhāḥ
pucchā-ghātena gagane
khaṇḍaṁ khaṇḍaṁ yayur ghanāḥ 5.2.2

“His footsteps made the great trees fall. The swishing of his tail broke apart the clouds in the sky.”

taṁ vīkṣya duḥsaha-javaṁ
gopa-gopī-gaṇā bhṛśām
bhayāturā maithilendra
śrī-kṛṣṇaṁ saraṇaṁ yayuḥ 5.2.3

“When they saw him fiercely charge at them, the terrified gopas and gopīs ran to Śrī Kṛṣṇa for shelter.”

mā bhaiṣṭety abhayaṁ dattvā
bhagavān vṛjinārdanaḥ
kaṭau pītāmbaraṁ baddhvā
hantuṁ daityaṁ pracakrame 5.2.4

“Saying, “Don’t be afraid”, the Lord removed their fears. Then, tightening the yellow sash at His waist, the Supreme Lord, the deliverer from troubles, went to kill the demon.”

hariṁ paścima-pādābhyāṁ
santatāḍa mahāsuraḥ
cālayan pṛthivīṁ rājan
nādayan vyoma-maṇḍalam 5.2.5

“O king, then, shaking the ground, and making the sky echo with his neighing, with his hind legs the great demon kicked Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

gṛhītvā pādayor daityaṁ
bhrāmayitvā bhujena khe
cikṣepa yojanaṁ kṛṣṇo
vātaḥ padmam ivoddhṛtam 5.2.6

“Grabbing the demon’s legs and whirling him around and around, with one arm Lord Kṛṣṇa threw the demon eight miles into the sky as a great wind uproots and throws a lotus flower.”

punar āgatavān so ’pi
krodha-pūrita-vigrahaḥ
pucchena śrī-hariṁ devaṁ
santatāḍa vrajāṅgane 5.2.7

“Filled with anger, the demon returned to the courtyards of Vraja and with his tail violently struck Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

pucche gṛhītvā taṁ kṛṣṇo
bhrāmayitvā bhujaujasā
yojanānāṁ śataṁ rājan
cikṣepa gagane balāt 5.2.8

“Grabbing that tail, and whirling the demon around and around, with the strength of His arms Lord Kṛṣṇa threw him eighty miles into the sky.”

ākāśāt patitaḥ so ’pi
kiñcid vyākula-mānasaḥ
samutthāya punar daityo
jagarja ghanavad balī 5.2.9

“When he fell from the sky, the powerful demon was somewhat disturbed at heart. Standing up again, he neighed like thunder.”

saṭā vidhunvan romāṇi
bālaṁ khe cālayan muhuḥ
mahīṁ vidārayan pādair
utpapāta hareḥ puraḥ 5.2.10

“Shaking his mane, swishing his tail in the sky, and breaking the ground with his hooves, the demon-horse jumped before Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

tatāḍa muṣṭinā taṁ vai
bhagavān madhusūdanaḥ
tasya muṣṭi-prahāreṇa
mūrcchito ghaṭikā-dvayam 5.2.11

“Then Lord Kṛṣṇa punched him, and the demon was unconscious for an hour.”

mastakena galoddeśe
samudhṛtya hariṁ hayaḥ
bhū-maṇḍalād utpapāta
gagane lakṣa-yojanam 5.2.12

“Then, grabbing His neck, the demon-horse pulled Lord Kṛṣṇa eight hundred thousand miles into the sky.”

tayor yuddham abhūd ghoraṁ
gagane prahara-dvayam
pādair dadbhiḥ saṭābhiś ca
puccha-tīkṣna-khurair nṛpa 5.2.13

“For six hours they fought fiercely. With his hooves, teeth, mane, and the sharp razor of his tail, the demon-horse attacked the Lord.”

gṛhītvā taṁ harir dorbhyāṁ
bhrāmayitvā tv itas tataḥ
ākāśāt pātayām āsa
kamaṇḍalum ivārbhakaḥ 5.2.14

“Suddenly grabbing him, and whirling him around and around, with both arms Lord Kṛṣṇa threw the demon to the earth as a child throws a kamaṇḍalu.”

bhujaṁ praveṣayām āsa
tan-mukhe bhagavān hariḥ
tasyodare gato bāhur
vavṛdhe rogavad bhṛśām 5.2.15

“Then Lord Kṛṣṇa thrust His arm in the demon’s mouth. Pushed into the demon’s belly, the arm expanded like an incurable disease.”

tadā tu leṇḍaṁ kṛtavān
ruddha-vāyur mahāsuraḥ
khaṇḍī-bhūtodaraḥ sadyo
mamāra haya-rūpa-dhṛk 5.2.16

“Then, passing stool and urine, his life breath stopped, and his stomach broken by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arm, the horse-demon suddenly died.”

dehād vinirgataḥ sadyo
mukuṭī kuṇḍalānvitaḥ
divya-rūpa-dharaṁ kṛṣṇaṁ
prāñjaliḥ praṇanāma ha 5.2.17

“From the demon’s body suddenly emerged a being wearing a crown and earrings. With folded hands he bowed down before glorious Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

śrī-kumuda uvāca
śakrasyānucaro ’haṁ vai
kumudo nāma mādhava
tejasvī rūpavān vīro
jiṣṇuś chatra-bhramiṁ dadhan 5.2.18

Śrī Kumuda said: “O Lord Kṛṣṇa, my name is Kumuda and I am a servant of King Indra. I am powerful, handsome, heroic, and glorious. I carry Indra’s parasol.”

vṛtrāsura-vadhe pūrvaṁ
brahma-hatyā-praśāntaye
yajñaṁ cakāra nākeśo
vājimedhaṁ kratūttamam 5.2.19

“When Vṛtrāsura was killed, to become free of the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa, Indra performed an aṣvamedha-yajña, the best of sacrifices.”

aśvamedha-hayaṁ śubhraṁ
śyāma-karṇaṁ mano-javam
tam ārurukṣur duṣṭo ’haṁ
corayitvā talaṁ gataḥ 5.2.20

“Yearning to ride it, I stole the splendid sacrificial horse, which had one black ear and ran as fast as the mind, and went to Talaloka.”

tato marud-gaṇair nītaṁ
pāśa-baddhaṁ mahā-khalam
saśāpa māṁ balārātis
tvaṁ rakṣo bhava durmate 5.2.21

“The Maruts bound me with ropes and brought me back. Indra cursed me, “Wretch! Become a demon!”

hayākṛtis te sambhūyād
bhūmau manvantara-dvayam
tac-chāpād adya mukto ’haṁ
sadyas tvat-sparśanāt prabho 5.2.22

““For two manvantaras you will have a horse’s form on the earth.” O Lord, today Your touch has freed me from that curse.”

kiṅkaraṁ kuru māṁ deva
tvad-aṅghrau lagna-mānasam
namas tubhyaṁ bhagavate
sarva-lokaika-sākṣiṇe 5.2.23

“O Lord please make me Your servant. My heart is fixed on Your feet. I offer my respectful obeisances to You, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the Supersoul who is the witness of all.”

Chapter 3 - Akrūra’s Arrival

śrī-nārada uvāca
akrūro ratham āruhya
kartuṁ kāryaṁ nṛpasya vai
praharṣito maithilendra
prayayau nanda-gokulam 5.3.1

Śrī Nārada said: “O king of Mithilā, happy to execute King Kaṁsa’s mission, Akrūra climbed on a chariot and went to Nanda’s Gokula.”

parāṁ bhaktiṁ hy upagataḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇe puruṣottame
evaṁ vicārayan buddhyā
pathi gacchan mahā-matiḥ 5.3.2

“As he traveled on the road, noble-hearted Akrūra meditated on Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He was filled with devotion for the Lord.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
kiṁ bhārate vā sukṛtaṁ kṛtaṁ mayā
niṣkāraṇaṁ dānam alaṁ kratūttamam
tīrthāṭanaṁ vā dvija-sevanaṁ śubhaṁ
yenādya drakṣyāmi hariṁ pareśvaram 5.3.3

Śrī Akrūra said: “How many pious deeds did I perform in the sacred land of Bhārata-varṣa? What unselfish charity did I give? How many great yajñas did I perform? How many pilgrimages did I make? How many brāhmaṇas did I diligently serve? What did I do that today I will be able to see Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead?”

tapaḥ su-taptaṁ kim alaṁ purā kṛtaṁ
sat-sevanaṁ bhakti-yutaṁ mayā kṛtam
yenaiva me darśanam adya durlabhaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇadevasya puro bhaviṣyati 5.3.4

“What great austerities did I perform in my previous births? What saintly devotees did I earnestly serve? What did I do that today I will become able to see Lord Kṛṣṇadeva, who is so difficult to see?”

teṣāṁ bhavo me sa-phalo mahī-tale
yan-netra-gāmī bhagavān sureśvaraḥ
kṛtvātha tad-darśanam adya durlabhaṁ
sadyaḥ kṛtārtho bhavitāsmi sarvataḥ 5.3.5

“My birth on this earth has now borne its perfect fruit, for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the master of the demigods, will come before my eyes. When I see Him, the Lord who is so difficult to see, my life will attain the greatest success.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
iti sañcintayan kṛṣṇaṁ
paśyañ chakunam uttamam
sandhyāyāṁ gokulaṁ prāpto
ratha-stho gandinī-sutaḥ 5.3.6

Śrī Nārada said: “Thinking of Kṛṣṇa in this way, and seeing a very auspicious omen, at sunset Akrūra entered Gokula.”

kṛṣṇa-pādābja-cihnāni
yavāṅkuśa-yutāni ca
tad-rāga-yuk-parāgāṇi
rajāṁsi sa dadarśa kau 5.3.7

“Then in the dust He saw Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus footprints marked with the signs of a barleycorn and elephant-goad and sprinkled with kuṅkuma powder.”

tad-darśanautsukya-bhakti-
bhāvānanda-samākulaḥ
rathāt samutpatya teṣu luṭhaṁś cāśru mumoca ca 5.3.8

“Shedding tears, and overcome with bliss, love, and great yearning to see the Lord, Akrūra jumped from the chariot and rooled about in those footprints.”

yeṣāṁ śrī-kṛṣṇadevasya
bhaktiḥ syād dhṛdi maithila
teṣām ā-brahmaṇaḥ sarvaṁ
tṛṇavaj jagataḥ sukham 5.3.9

“O king of Mithilā, they who in their hearts love Kṛṣṇa think all this material world’s happiness, even up Brahmā’s happiness, is like a a handful of dry straw.”

rathārūḍhas tato ’krūraḥ
kṣaṇān nanda-puraṁ gataḥ
ghoseṣu sa-balaṁ kṛṣṇaṁ
āgacchantaṁ dadarśa ha 5.3.10

“Riding on his chariot, in a moment Akrūra entered Nanda’s village and saw Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma approaching him.”

devau purāṇau puruṣau pareśau
padmekṣaṇau śyāmala-gaura-varṇau
yathendranīla-dhvaja-vajra-śailau
samāśritau tau pathi rāma-kṛṣṇau
bālārka-maulī vasanaṁ taḍid-dyutī
varṣāśaran-megha-rucaṁ dadhānau
dṛṣṭvā sa tūrṇaṁ sva-rathād gato ’dhao
tayor nato bhakti-yutaḥ papāta 5.3.11-12

“Seeing on the pathway the two ancient Supreme Persons, Their eyes lotus flowers, Their complexions fair and dark like a sapphire flag and a diamond mountain, wearing crowns like the rising sun and garments splendid as lightning and autumn clouds, Akrūra, overcome with love, jumped from his chariot and fell down before Them to offer his respects.”

tad-ānanaṁ bāṣpa-kulākulekṣaṇaṁ
romañcitaṁ vīkṣya hariḥ pareśvaraḥ
dorbhyāṁ samutthāpya ghṛṇāturo ’śru
mumoca bhaktaṁ parirabhya mādhavaḥ 5.3.13

“Gazing at Akrūra’s face and seeing his eyes filled with tears and the hairs of his body erect with joy, Lord Kṛṣṇa, overcome with feelings of compassion, and shedding tears Himself, picked Akrūra up and with both arms embraced His devotee.”

evaṁ militvā sa-balaś ca taṁ hariḥ
sadyaḥ samānīya varāsanaṁ dadau
nivedya gāṁ cātithaye su-bhojanaṁ
rasāvṛtaṁ prema-yuto hy upāharat 5.3.14

“Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma offered him a comfortable seat, welcomed him with eloquent words, and offered Their guest a delicious meal.”

tam āha nandaḥ parirabhya dorbhyāṁ
aho kathaṁ jīvasi kaṁsa-rājye
gata-trapo yo nijaghāna balān
svasuḥ kathaṁ so ’nya-janeṣu mohī 5.3.15

“Nanda embraced Akrūra with both arms and said, “You are kind to others. How can you live in the kingdom of Kaṁsa, who killed the sons of his own sister?”

gṛhaṁ gate nanda-vare haris taṁ
papraccha sarvaṁ kuśalaṁ sva-pitroḥ
tathā yadūnāṁ kila bāndhavānāṁ
kaṁsasya sarvaṁ viparīta-buddhim 5.3.16

“When Nanda had gone, Kṛṣṇa asked about the welfare of His father and His kinsmen, the Yādavas, and about the wicked deeds of Kaṁsa.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
paraśvo ’hani he deva
hantuṁ śauriṁ samudyataḥ
khaḍga-pāṇir bhoja-rājo
nāradena nivāritaḥ 5.3.17

Śrī Akrūra said: “The day before yesterday Kaṁsa, sword in hand, was about to kill Vasudeva until Nārada Muni stopped him.”

duḥkhitāḥ bāndhavāḥ sarve
yādavā bhaya-vihvalāḥ
sa-kuṭumbāḥ kaṁsa-bhayād
bhūman deśāntaraṁ gatāḥ 5.3.18

“O Lord, unhappy and terrified of Kṛṣṇa, your kinsmen, the Yādavas, have fled with their families to other countries.”

adyaiva yādavān hantuṁ
devān jetuṁ samudyataḥ
anyat kim api kau kartuṁ
icchate daitya-rāḍ balī 5.3.19

“Now the powerful demon-king Kaṁsa wants to kill the Yādavas, conquer the demigods, and perform more atrocities on this earth.”

tasmād bhavadbhyāṁ gantavyaṁ
kuśalaṁ kartum avyayam
bhavantau hi vinā kāryaṁ
kiñcin na syāt satāṁ prabhu 5.3.20

“Therefore You two Lords should go there and bring eternal auspiciousness to Your devotees. O my Lords, no one but You can bring auspiciousness to Your devotees.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha tasya vacaḥ śrutvā
sa-balo bhagavān hariḥ
nanda-rāja-matenāha
gopān kārya-karān idam 5.3.21

“After hearing Akrūra’s words, and after conferring with Balarāma and King Nanda, Lord Kṛṣṇa addressed the gopas, who were eager to follow His command.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
nanda-rājo ’pi sa-balo
vṛddhair gopa-gaṇair aham
nandā navopanandāś ca
tathā ṣaḍ vṛṣabhānavaḥ
mathurāṁ tu gamiṣyanti
sarve prātaḥ samutthitāḥ
sarve tu gorasaṁ tasmād
dadhi-dugdha-ghṛtādikam 5.3.22-23

The Blessed Lord said: “King Nanda, Balarāma, the nine Nandas and Upanandas, the six Vṛṣabhānus, and all the adult gopas will rise early tommorrow morning and, taking gifts of milk, yogurt, ghee, and other milk products, will go to Mathurā City.”

gṛhītvaikātra kartavyaṁ
sopāyanam ataḥ param
rathāṁś ca śakaṭaiḥ sārdhaṁ
samarthān kurutāśu vai 5.3.24

“These gifts should now be placed on all the chariots and wagons.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
iti śrutvā kārya-kārā
gopāḥ sarve gṛhe gṛhe
śṛṇvantīnāṁ gopikānāṁ
ūcuḥ sarvaṁ yathoditam 5.3.25

Śrī Nārada said: “When they heard these words, all the gopas repeated them to the gopīs in home after home.”

tac chrutvodvigna-hṛdayā
gopyo viraha-vihvalāḥ
parasparaṁ vākyam ūcuḥ
sarvās tā hi gṛhe gṛhe 5.3.26

“Agitated at heart to hear these words, and fearing a separation from Lord Kṛṣṇa, in home after home all the gopīs talked among themselves.”

prasthānasya ca varteyaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇasya mahātmanaḥ
vṛṣabhānu-varasyāpi
gṛhe prāptā nṛpeśvara 5.3.27

“O king of kings, the news that Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, would go on a journey, also entered the home of King Vṛṣabhānu.”

gamiṣyato bhārtur atīva-duḥkhitāḥ
śrutvātha vārtāṁ sadasi hy ākāśmāt
samprāpa mūrchaṁ vṛṣabhānu-nandinī
rambheva bhūmau patitā marud-dhatā 5.3.28

“When in the royal assembly She heard that Her master would go on a journey, King Vṛṣabhānu’s daughter, Śrī Rādhā, at once fainted. She was like a banana tree struck by a great wind.”

kāścit parimlāna-mukha-śriyo ’bhavan
prakaṅkanī-bhūta-karāṅgulīyakāḥ
sadyaḥ ślathad-bhūṣaṇa-keśa-bāndhanās
citrārpitārambhā ivāvatasthire 5.3.29

“Some gopīs, the beauty of their faces suddenly withered away, the rings slipping like bracelets from their suddenly emaciated fingers, their ornaments falling, and their hair dishevelled, were stunned and motionless as if they were painted pictures.”

śrī-kṛṣṇa govinda hare murāre
kāścid vadantyaḥ sva-gṛhe ’ti-vihvalāḥ
visṛjya karmāṇi gṛhasya sarvato
yogīva cānanda-gatā nṛpeśvara 5.3.30

“O king of kings, some gopīs became very upset. In their homes they called out, “O Śrī Kṛṣṇa! O Govinda! O Hari! O Murāri!” Neglecting their household duties they sat down like yogīs fixed in a trance of bliss.”

kāścit samarthas tu parasparaṁ vacaḥ
sametya rājan yugapat sakhī-janam
ūcuḥ skhalad-gadgada-kaṇṭha-vācaḥ
svataḥ sravad-bāṣpa-kalā-vahad-dṛśaḥ 5.3.31

“O king, some met and talked among themselves, their throats choked with stuttering words and their eyes with streams of tears.”

śrī-gopya ūcuḥ
aho ’ti-nirmohi-janasya citraṁ
paraṁ caritraṁ gadituṁ na yogyam
mukhena cānyaṁ hṛdi bhavyam anyad
devo na jānāti kuto manuṣyaḥ 5.3.32

The gopīs said: “Even the philosophers free of all illusion cannot with their mouths describe the wonderful transcendental pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa or meditate on them in their hearts. Even the great demigods cannot understand Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. How can ordinary human beings understand them?”

Chapter 4 - The Journey to Śrī Mathurā

śrī-nārada uvāca
rājann evaṁ vadantīnāṁ
gopīnāṁ virahaṁ param
vijñāya bhagavān devaḥ
śīghraṁ tāsāṁ gṛhān yayau 5.4.1

Śrī Nārada said: “Aware of the anguish felt by the gopīs that spoke these words, the Supreme Personality of Godhead quickly went to their homes.”

yāvantyo yoṣito rājaṁs
tāvad rūpa-dharo hariḥ
svayaṁ sambodhayām āsa
vāgbhiḥ sarvāḥ pṛthak pṛthak 5.4.2

“Expanding into as many forms as there were gopīs, Lord Kṛṣṇa spoke words of instruction to each gopī individually.”

śrī-rādhā-mandiraṁ gatvā
dṛṣṭvā rādhāṁ ca mūrchitam
rahaḥ-sthitaṁ sakhī-saṅge
nanāda muralīṁ kalām 5.4.3

“Going to Śrī Rādhā’s palace and seeing Rādhā fallen unconscious, in a secluded place, and in the company of Her friends, Lord Kṛṣṇa played sweet and soft notes on His flute.”

śrutvā vaṁśī-dhvaniṁ rādhā
sahasotthāya cāturā
netra unmīlya dadṛśe
śrī-govindaṁ samāgatam 5.4.4

“When She heard the flute-music, Rādhā became conscious again. Agitated, She opened Her eyes and saw that Lord Kṛṣṇa had come.”

padminīva gatānandaṁ
padminī padminī-patim
vīkṣyotthāyāgatā tasmai
sādareṇāsanaṁ dadau 5.4.5

“She gazed at Him as a lotus flower gazes at the sun. Approaching Him, She respectfully offered Him a seat.”

aśru-pūrṇa-mukhīṁ dīnāṁ
rādhāṁ kamala-locanām
śocantīṁ bhagavān āha
megha-gambhīrayā girā 5.4.6

“Gazing at poor, weeping, lotus-eyed Rādhā, Her face filled with tears, the Supreme Personality of Godhead spoke with words deep as thunder.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
vimanās tvaṁ kathaṁ bhadre
mā śocaṁ kuru rādhike
athavā gantu-kāmaṁ māṁ
śrutvāsi virahāturā 5.4.7

The Blessed Lord said: “Why are You unhappy, beautiful one? O Rādhā, please don’t cry. Are You unhappy because You heard that I want to go away?”

bhuvo bhārāvatārāya
kaṁsādīnāṁ vadhāya ca
brahmaṇā prārthitaḥ sākṣāj
jāto ’haṁ vai tvayā saha 5.4.8

“To remove the earth’s burden and to kill the demons headed by Kaṁsa, on Brahmā’s request I have taken birth here with You as My companion.”

mathurāṁ hi gamiṣyāmi
hariṣyāmi bhuvo bharam
śīghram atrāgamiṣyāmi
kariṣyāmi śubhaṁ tava 5.4.9

“I will go to Mathurā and remove the earth’s burden. Then I will quickly return here and make You happy.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktavantaṁ jagad-īśvaraṁ hariṁ
rādhā patiṁ prāha viyoga-vihvalā
dāvāgninā dāva-lateva mūrchitā
su-kampa-romañcita-bhāva-samvṛtā 5.4.10

Śrī Nārada said: “Then, filled with love, trembling, and Her bodily hairs erect, Rādhā, who was like a flowering vine caught fire in a conflagration of anxiety about Their separation, spoke to Her master, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

śrī-rādhovāca
bhuvo bharaṁ hartum alaṁ purīṁ vraja
kṛtaṁ paraṁ me śapathaṁ śṛṇu tvataḥ
gate tvayi prāṇa-pate ca vigrahaṁ
kadācid atraiva na dhārayāmy aham 5.4.11

Śrī Rādhā said: “Go to the city to remove the earth’s burden. But please hear the vow I make. O master of My life, while You are gone I will not keep this body alive.”

yadāttha me tvaṁ śapathaṁ na manyase
dvitīya-vāraṁ pradadāmi vāk-pathaṁ
prāṇo ’dhare gantum atīva-vihvalaḥ
karpūra-dhūleḥ kaṇavad gamiṣyati 5.4.12

“If You no not believe I will keep this vow, I say it for a second time: The anxious breath that appears on these lips will be extinguished like a speck of camphor devoured by a flame.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
vacanaṁ me sva-nigamaṁ
dūrī-kartuṁ kṣamo ’smy aham
bhaktānāṁ vacanaṁ rādhe
dūrī-kartuṁ na ca kṣamaḥ 5.4.13

The Blessed Lord said: “I can break My own vow, but I have no power to break the vow of My devotees.”

śrīdāma-śāpāt pūrvasmād
goloke kalahān mama
śata-varṣaṁ te viyogo
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 5.4.14

“Because of a quarrel in Goloka, Śrīdāmā cursed You to be separated fgrom Me. You must be separated from Me for a hundred years. Of this there is no doubt.”

mā śokaṁ kuru kalyāṇi
varaṁ me smara rādhike
māsaṁ māsaṁ viyoge te
darśanaṁ me bhaviṣyati 5.4.15

“Beautiful one, don’t cry. O Rādhā, remember Me always as Your lover. Month after month You will still see Me, even though We are separated.”

śrī-rādhovāca
māsaṁ prati viyoge me
dātuṁ svaṁ darśanaṁ hare
cen nāgamiṣyasi tadā-
sūn duḥkhāt santyajāmy aham 5.4.16

Śrī Rādhā said: “If every month You do not return to let Me see You I will grieve and give up My life.”

lokābhirāma jana-bhūṣaṇa viśva-dīpa
kandarpa-mohana jagad-vṛjinārti-hārin
ānanda-kanda yadu-nandana nanda-sūno
adyāgamasya śapathaṁ kuru me puras tvam 5.4.17

“O happiness of the worlds, O ornament of the people, O lamp lighting the universe, O youth more handsome than Kāmadeva, O Lord who removes the world’s sufferings, O root from which all bliss has blossomed, O descendent of King Yadu, O son of Nanda, promise Me that You will return.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
rambhoru māsaṁ prati te viyoge
cen nāgamiṣye śapathaṁ gavāṁ me
niḥsaṁśayaṁ niṣkapaṭaṁ vacas tvaṁ
avehi rādhe kathitaṁ mayā yat 5.4.18

The Blessed Lord said: “O girl with thighs graceful as banana trees, every month I will return. I vow this on all My cows. O Rādhā, please believe that My words are not a lie.”

yo mitratāṁ niṣkapaṭaṁ karoti
niṣkāraṇo dhanyatamaḥ sa eva
vidhāya maitrīṁ kapaṭaṁ vidadhyāt
taṁ lampaṭaṁ hetu-paṭaṁ naṭaṁ dhik 5.4.19

“An unselfish and sincere friend is the most fortunate of persons. Fie on one who is only an actor pretending to be a friend!”

karmendriyāṇīha yathā rasādiṁs
tathā sa-kāmā munayaḥ sukhaṁ yat
manāṅ na jānanti hi nairapekṣaṁ
gūḍhaṁ paraṁ nirguṇa-lakṣaṇaṁ tat 5.4.20

“Philosophers that still desire the objects of the material senses cannot at all understand the great secret of renunciation, a secret untouched by the three modes of nature.”

jānanti santaḥ sama-darśino ye
dāntā mahāntaḥ kila nairapekṣāḥ
te nairapekṣyaṁ paramaṁ sukhaṁ me
jñānendriyādīni yathā rasādīn 5.4.21

“Saintly devotees, who control their senses and see everyone equally, know the great happiness of renunciation. They taste Me with their tongues and the directly experience Me with their other senses.”

sarvaṁ hi bhāvaṁ manasaḥ parasparaṁ
na hy ekato bhāmini jāyate tataḥ
premaiva kartavyam ato mayi svataḥ
premṇā samānaṁ bhuvi nāsti kiñcit 5.4.22

“O beautiful one, they love Me with all their hearts. The living entities should love Me. In this world nothing is equal to love for Me.”

yathā hi bhāṇḍīra-vaṭe manoratho
babhūva rādhe hi tathā bhaviṣyati
ahaitukaṁ prema ca sadbhir āśritaṁ
tac cāpi santaḥ kila nirguṇaṁ viduḥ 5.4.23

“O Rādhā, as in the past I fulfilled Your desires in Bhāṇḍīravana forest, so in the future I will fulfill them again. The saintly devotees love Me selflessly. They stand on the spiritual platform, above the three modes of material nature.”

ye rādhikāyāṁ tvayi keśave mayi
bhedaṁ na kurvanti hi dugdha-sauklyavat
ta eva me brahma-padaṁ prayānti tad
ahaituka-sphurjita-bhakti-lakṣaṇāḥ 5.4.24

“They who know that as milk and its quality of whiteness cannot be separated, so there is no difference between You, Rādhā, and Me, Kṛṣṇa, are My selfless devotees. They go to My transcendental abode.”

ye rādhikāyāṁ tvayi keśave mayi
paśyanti bhedaṁ ku-dhiyo narā bhuvi
te kāla-sūtraṁ prapatanti duḥkhitā
rambhoru yāvat kila candra-bhāskarau 5.4.25

“They who see a difference etween You, Rādhā, and Me, Kṛṣṇa, are fools. O girl with thighs graceful as banana trees, they fall into the line of time and suffer there for as long as the sun and the moon shine in the sky.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evam āśvāsya tāṁ rādhāṁ
sarvaṁ gopī-gaṇaṁ tathā
āyayau nanda-bhavanaṁ
bhagavān naya-kovidaḥ 5.4.26

Śrī Nārada said: “After thus comforting Rādhā and the gopīs, the Lord, who is the wisest of philosophers, returned to Nanda’s palace.”

atha sūryodaye jāte
nandādyāḥ śakaṭair balim
nītvā rathān samāruhya
sarve śrī-mathurāṁ yayuḥ 5.4.27

“The next morning at sunrise Nanda and all the gopas put their offerings in many wagons, themselves climbed onto many chariots, and went to Mathurā.”

āruhya rāma-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ
svaṁ rathaṁ gandinī-sutaḥ
prayāṇam akarod rājan
mathurāṁ draṣṭum udyataḥ 5.4.28

“O king, Akrūra climbed on his chariot and with Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma went to see Mathurā.”

koṭisaḥ koṭiśo gopyo
mārge mārge samāsthitāḥ
paśyantyas tan-nirgamanaṁ
krodhāḍhyā moha-vihvalāḥ 5.4.29

“Angry and bewildered, millions and millions of gopīss stood on every path, staring at the Lord’s departure.”

krūra krūreti cākrūraṁ
vadantyaḥ pāruṣaṁ vacaḥ
rurudhuḥ sarvato yānaṁ
yathārkaṁ sa-rathaṁ ghanaḥ 5.4.30

“Calling out, “Cruel! Cruel!” they jeered Akrūra. As clouds block the sun-god’s chariot, they blocked every path”

akrūrasya rathaṁ rājan
nijaghnur yaṣṭibhir bhṛśām
aśvāṁs tathā sārathiṁ ca
bhagavad-virahāturāḥ 5.4.31

“O king, anguished by their imminent separation from the Lord, with sticks the gopīs whipped Akrūra’s chariot, horses, and chariot driver.”

aśvās tatra samutpetus
tāḍitās ta itas tataḥ
gopī-dvy-aṅguli-ghātena
sārathiḥ patito rathāt 5.4.32

“The whipped horses suddenly bolted, running here and there. Pushed by two gopī fingers, the charioteer fell from the chariot.”

vihāya lajjāṁ lokasya
samākṛṣya rathād balāt
kaṅkaṇais teḍur akrūraṁ
paśyatoḥ kṛṣṇa-rāmayoḥ 5.4.33

“Throwing their shyness far away, they dragged Akrūra from the chariot and beat him with their bracelets as Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma passively looked on.”

gopī-yūtha-balaṁ dṛṣṭvā
sa-balo bhagavān hariḥ
gopīḥ sambodhayām āsa
rakṣitvā gandinī-sutam 5.4.34

“Seeing how strong the gopīs were, Lord Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma interceded on Akrūra’s behalf and protected him. Lord Kṛṣṇa said to the gopīs:”

sandhyāyām āgamiṣyāmi
mā śokaṁ kurutāṅganāḥ
paśyataś cāsya mad-dhāsyaṁ
mā kurvantu vrajaukasaḥ 5.4.35

“Girls, don’t cry. At sunset I will return. The people of Vraja should not trouble Akrūra.”

ity evam uktvā sarathaḥ samāgato
’krūreṇa kṛṣṇo baladeva-samyutaḥ
turaṅgamair vegamayair manoharair
yayau purīṁ yādava-vṛnda-maṇḍitam 5.4.36

“After speaking these words, Lord Kṛṣṇa, accompanied by Balarāma and Akrūra, journeyed in the chariot pulled by many fast and handsome horses to the great city Mathurā, which had the Yādavas as its splendid decoration.”

Chapter 5 - Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s Entrance Into Mathurā

śrī-nārada uvāca
harir akrūra-rāmābhyāṁ
mathuropavanaṁ gataḥ
yamunā-nikaṭaṁ sthitvā
vari pītvā rathaṁ yayau 5.5.1

Śrī Nārada said: “Accompanied by Balarāma and Akrūra, Kṛṣṇa went to Mathurā’s gardens by the Yamunā’s shore, drank some water, and returned to the chariot.”

akrūras tāv anujñāpya
snātuṁ śrī-yamunāṁ gataḥ
nitya-naimittikaṁ kartuṁ
viveśa vimale jale 5.5.2

“Atter taking permission from Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, Akrūra went to the Yamunā and entered the water to take his regular bath.”

jale cāgadha-gambhīre
mahā-varta-samākule
dadarśa rāma-kṛṣṇau tau
vadantau gandinī-sutaḥ 5.5.3

“In the deep and turbulent water Akrūra saw Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma conversing.”

vismitas tau rathe ’paśyat
punar vāri sthitau nṛpa
dadarśa tatra sarpendraṁ
kuṇḍalī-bhūtam āsthitam 5.5.4

“O king, when he looked and saw Them both sitting on the chariot, Akrūra was very surprised. He looked again into the water and saw Ananta Śeṣa coiled again and again, . . .”

tasyotsaṅge mahā-lokaṁ
golokaṁ loka-vanditam
govardhanādriṁ yamunāṁ
vṛndāraṇyaṁ manoharam 5.5.5

“On Śeṣa’s lap he saw Vṛndāvana forest, the Yamunā, Govardhana Hill, and the transcendental realm of Goloka, which is worshiped by all the worlds.”

asaṅkhya-koṭi-mārtaṇḍa-
jyotiṣaṁ maṇḍalaṁ prabhum
paripūrṇatamaṁ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇaṁ puruṣottamam 5.5.6

“On Śeṣa’s lap Akrūra also saw Lord Kṛṣṇa, the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead, glorious as countless millions of suns, . . .”

koṭi-manmatha-lāvaṇyaṁ
rāsa-maṇḍala-madhya-gam
rādhayā sahitaṁ devaṁ
tatrākrūro dadarśa ha 5.5.7

“. . . and more handsome than millions of Kāmadevas, standing with Rādhā in the rāsa-dance circle.”

jñātvā kṛṣṇaṁ paraṁ brahma
natvā natvā punaḥ punaḥ
kṛtāñjali-puṭo ’krūraḥ
stutiṁ cakre ’ti-harṣitaḥ 5.5.8

“Aware that Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Akrūra bowed down again and again and again. Filled with joy, he folded his hands and spoke many prayers.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
namaḥ śrī-kṛṣṇacandrāya
paripūrṇatamāya ca
asaṅkhyāṇḍādhipataye
goloka-pataye namaḥ 5.5.9

Śrī Akrūra said: “Obeisances to Śrī Kṛṣṇacandra, the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead! Obeisances to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the master of numberless universes, the master of Goloka!”

śrī-rādhā-pataye tubhyaṁ
vrajādhīśāya te namaḥ
namaḥ śrī-nanda-putrāya
yaśodā-nandanāya ca 5.5.10

“Obeisances to You, the master of Rādhā and the king of Vraja! Obeisances to You, the son of Nanda and Yaśodā!”

devakī-suta govinda
vāsudeva jāgat-pate
yadūttama jagan-nātha
pāhi māṁ puruṣottama 5.5.11

“O son of Devakī and Vasudeva, O Govinda, O best of the Yādavas, O master of the universes, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, please protect me!”

vāṇī sadā te guṇa-varṇane syāt
karṇau kathāyāṁ mama doś ca karmaṇi
manaḥ sadā tvac-caraṇāravindayor
dṛśau sphurad-dhāma-viveśa-darśane 5.5.12

“May my words always describe Your transcendental qualities, my ears always hear descriptions of You, my arms always work for You, my mind always think of Your lotus feet, and my eyes always see the splendid glory of Your transcendental form.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ saṁstuvatas tasya
paśyato vismitasya ca
tatraivāntardadhe kṛṣṇaḥ
sa-loko bhagavān prabhuḥ 5.5.13

Śrī Nārada said: “As astonished Akrūra was speaking these prayers, the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa in His transcendental realm suddenly disappeared.”

natvā taṁ ca tadākrūraḥ
kṛtvā naimittikaṁ vidhim
jñātvā kṛṣṇaṁ paraṁ brahma
vismito ratham āyayau 5.5.14

“Bowing down, finishing his regular duties, and now acutely aware of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s divinity, astonished Akrūra returned to the chariot.”

dinātyaye rāma-kṛṣṇāv
anayad gandinī-sutaḥ
rathena vāyu-vegena
snigdha-gambhīra-nādinā 5.5.15

“Driving a chariot that rumbled like thunder and went as fast as the wind, at the day’s end Akrūra took Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma to the gardens outside Mathurā.”

purasyopavane tatra
vīkṣya nandaṁ yadūttamaḥ
akrūraṁ prāha vihasan
megha-gambhīrayā girā 5.5.16

“When He saw Nanda there in Mathurā’s gardens, Lord Kṛṣṇa smiled and spoke to Akrūra with words like thunder.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
mathurāyāṁ hi gantavyaṁ
bhavatā sva-rathena vai
gopālaiḥ sahitaḥ paścād
āgamiṣyāmi mānada 5.5.17

The Blessed Lord said: “Humble Akrūra, please take your chariot inside Mathurā City. Accompanied by the gopas, I will enter the city later.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
deva-deva jagan-nātha
govinda puruṣottama
sahāgrajaḥ sa-gopālo
gaccha me mandiraṁ prabho 5.5.18

Śrī Akrūra said: “O master of the demigods, O master of the universes, O Govinda, O Supreme Person, taking with You Your elder brother and Your gopa friends, please agree to visit my home.”

padāravinda-rajasā
pavitrī-kuru mad-gṛham
tvaṁ vinā na gamiṣyāmi
mandiraṁ svaṁ jāgat-pate 5.5.19

“Please purify my home with the pollen of Your lotus feet. O master of the worlds, I will not return to my home unless I can take You with me.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
gṛhaṁ tavāgamiṣyāmi
hatvā vai yādavāhitam
sa-balo bandhavaiḥ sārdhaṁ
kariṣyāmi tava priyam 5.5.20

The Blessed Lord said: “After I have killed the king that harms the Yādavas, I will visit your home. Accompanied by Balarāma and My kindsmen, I will satisfy your desire.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha tatra sthite kṛṣṇe
so ’krūro mathurāṁ gataḥ
nivedya cedaṁ kaṁśāya
tataḥ sva-bhavanaṁ yayau 5.5.21

Śrī Nārada said: “Leaving Lord Kṛṣṇa there, Akrūra entered Mathurā, informed King Kaṁsa of what had happened, and then returned to his home.”

athāparāhne sa-balaṁ
govindaṁ bālakaiḥ purīm
draṣṭum abhyuditaṁ vīkṣya
nando vākyam athābravīt 5.5.22

“Noticing that afternoon that Kṛṣṇa, Balarāma, and the boys were eager to see Mathurā City, Nanda spoke the following words.”

ārjavena purīṁ vīkṣyā-
gantavyaṁ bhavatā kila
na gokulaṁ viddhi cainaṁ
kaṁsa-rājye mahā-bhaye 5.5.23

“Look at the city and then quickly return. Know that this is not Gokula. This is the fearful kingdom of Kaṁsa.”

tathāstu coktvā bhagavān
vṛddhair nanda-praṇoditaiḥ
gopālair bālakaiḥ sārdhaṁ
sa-balo gatavān purīm 5.5.24

“Saying, “So be it”, and accompanied by Balarāma, the boys, and some adult gopas sent by Nanda, Lord Kṛṣṇa entered Mathurā City, . . .”

prāsādair gagana-sparśair
hema-ratna-khacid-gṛhaiḥ
śobhitāṁ durga-samyuktāṁ
devadhānīm iva sthitām 5.5.25

“. . . which was surrounded by high walls, and filled with mansions of gold and jewels and great palaces that touched the sky, which was like a great city in the celestial planets, . . .”

kālindīṁ ratna-sopānaiś
calad-ūrmi-kutūhalaiḥ
alakām iva śobhāḍhyāṁ
divya-nārī-narair yutam 5.5.26

“. . . which was filled with handsome men and beautiful women, and decorated with jewel staircases leading down to the Yamunā’s turbulent waves splendid as black locks of hair.”

prekṣan chrī-mathurāṁ kṛṣṇo
dhanināṁ mandirāṇi ca
paśyan gopālakaiḥ sārdhaṁ
rāja-mārgaṁ viveśa ha 5.5.27

“As they gazed at the palaces of the wealthy and the other opulences of Mathurā, Kṛṣṇa and the gopas started to walk on the king’s road.”

śrutvāgataṁ taṁ vasudeva-nandanaṁ
bahuśrutā vai mathurā-purāṅganāḥ
tyaktvātha karmāṇi visṛjya tāḥ śiśūn
draṣṭuṁ vyadhāvann udadhiṁ yathāpagāḥ 5.5.28

“Hearing that Vasudeva’s son had come, the learned women of Mathurā at once left their children and all their household duties and ran to see Kṛṣṇa as rivers run into the ocean.”

kāścit tu harmyāt kila jāla-deśāt
kuḍyāt tu kaścit paṭato gavākṣāt
vinirgatā dvāra-kapāṭa-deśāt
tac cātvarāt taṁ dadṛśuḥ purandhryaḥ 5.5.29

“Some from palaces, some from behind latticeworks, some from the tops of walls, some from behind curtains, some from windows, some from doorways, and some from courtyards, the women of Mathurā gazed at Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

ekaṁ calat-kuntalam ānane sve
kim agra-gānāṁ tu manāṁsi hartum
paścāt kṛtaṁ mauli-tale dadhānaṁ
kiṁ pṛṣṭha-gānāṁ haraṇaṁ dvitīyam
pītāmbarārdhaṁ balinaṁ sphurat kaṭāv
ardhaṁ tad-aṁse jalade yathā taḍit
padmaṁ kare sve hṛdi vaijayantīṁ
srajaṁ dadhānaṁ vasudeva-nandanam
vilokya sarvā mumuhuḥ pura-striyo
vilola-pāṭhīna-navīna-kuṇḍalam
bālārka-hemāṅgada-bāhu-maṇḍalaṁ
rājann asaṅkhyāṇḍa-patiṁ parāt param 5.5.30-32

“O king, as they gazed at Lord Kṛṣṇa, the master of numberless universes, His face decorated in the front with moving locks of hair and in the back with a glittering crown, wearing a yellow cloth wrapped around His waist and over His shoulder, splendid as a dark cloud with a lightning flash, a vaijayantī galrand on His chest and a lotus in His hand, wearing swinging shark-shaped earrings and golden armlets splendid as the rising sun, and stealing their hearts as He walked before Him, all the women of Mathurā City fell hopelessly in love with Him.”

śrī-purandhrya ūcuḥ
aho vṛndāvanaṁ ramyaṁ
yatra sannihito hy ayam
dhanyā gopa-gaṇāḥ sarve
paśyanty enaṁ manoharam 5.5.33

The women of Mathurā City said: “Ah, all the gopas in beautiful Vṛndāvana are very fortunate again and again to see such a handsome person.”

dhanyā gopa-ramaṇyas tās
tābhiḥ kiṁ sukṛtaṁ kṛtam
pibanti yā rāsa-raṅge
muhuś cāsyādharāmṛtam 5.5.34

“The gopīs are very fortunate. What pious deeds must they have performed that in the rāsa-dance they could again and again drink the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s lips?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
rāja-mārge raṅga-kāraṁ
rajakaṁ yāntam unmadam
gopālānumatenaiva
prāha taṁ madhusūdanaḥ 5.5.35

Śrī Nārada said: “To please the gopas, Lord Kṛṣṇa spoke the following words to a washerman and cloth-dyer that was frantically going on the king’s road.”

dehi no mitra vāsāṁsi
rucirāṇi mahā-mate
dātus te hi paraṁ sreyo
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 5.5.36

“O noble hearted friend, give these beautiful graments to us. If you give them, you will get a great gift in return. Of this there is no doubt.”

prajvalan kṛṣṇa-vākyena
ghṛtenāgnir yathā bhṛśām
kaṁsa-bhṛtyo mahā-duṣṭaḥ
prāhedaṁ pathi mādhavam 5.5.37

“Inflamed by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s words as fire is inflamed by ghee, the washerman, who was a great demon and servant of Kaṁsa, spoke to Lord Kṛṣṇa in the middle of the road.”

rajaka uvāca
idṛśāny eva vastrāṇi
pitṛbhir vaḥ pitāmahaiḥ
dhāritāni kim udvṛttās
te tu kaupīna-dhārakāḥ 5.5.38

The washerman said: “O proud wearers of kaupīnas, did Your fathers or grandfathers ever wear clothes like these?”

yātāśu vanyā nagarāt
sarve vai jīvitecchayā
kārāgāre kārayāmi
yuṣmān vastra-harān aham 5.5.39

“O uncivilized people who live in the forest, if you value your lives, then flee from this city! If you do not, then I will have you put in jail for stealing my garments.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ pravadatas tasya
rajakasya yadūttamaḥ
jahāra mastakaṁ sadyaḥ
karāgreṇaiva līlayā 5.5.40

Śrī Nārada said: “As soon as these words were spoken, with the edge of His hand Lord Kṛṣṇa playfully beheaded the washerman.”

taj-jyotiḥ śrī-ghana-śyāme
līnaṁ jātaṁ videha-rāṭ
sadyas tad-anugāḥ sarve
vāsaḥ-kośān visṛjya vai
dudruvuḥ sarvato rājan
śarat-kāle yathā ghanāḥ
gṛhītvātma-priye vastre
sthitayo rāma-kṛṣṇayaoḥ 5.5.41-42

“O king of Videha, then an effulgence left the washerman’s dead body and entered the dark cloud of Lord Kṛṣṇa. At once the washerman’s assistants dropped the great treasury of opulent gamrnets and fled in all directions as monsoon clouds flee with the coming of autumn. Calmly standing there, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma took what they liked from the pile of garments.”

jagṛhur gopa-bālās te
rāja-mārga-janā api
tad-dhāraṇāvido bālā
vāsāṁsi rucirāṇi ca asta-vyastaṁ paridadhuḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇasya prapaśyataḥ 5.5.43

“The gopa boys took what they wanted, and other boys walking on the king’s road, unaware of what had happened, also took from the pile of beautiful clothes as Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma placidly looked on.”

vīkṣya tau vāyakaḥ kaścic
chrī-kṛṣṇa-baladevayoḥ
vicitra-varṇair vāsobhir
divyaṁ veṣaṁ cakāra ha 5.5.44

“Then a weaver noticed Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma and at once dressed Them in beautiful and colorful garments.”

tathānyeṣāṁ śiśūnāṁ ca
yathā-yogyaṁ vidhāya saḥ
rājan paramayā bhaktyā
punaḥ kṛṣṇaṁ dadarśa ha 5.5.45

“He also dressed the other gopa boys. Then with great devotion he again gazed at Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

Chapter 6 - Seeing Śrī Mathurā

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha gopālakaiḥ sārdhaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇo nanda-nandanaḥ
gṛhaṁ jāgama sa-balaḥ
sudāmno dāma-mālinaḥ 5.6.1

“Then, accompanied by Balarāma and the gopas, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda, went to the florist Sudāmā’s home.”

dṛṣṭvā tau sa samutthāya
namaskṛtya kṛtāñjaliḥ
puṣpa-siṁhāsane sthāpya
prāha gadgadayā girā 5.6.2

“When he saw Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, Sudāmā at once stood up and then bowed down with folded hands. Placing Them on a flower throne, he stuttered words choked with emotion.”

śrī-sudāmovāca
dhanyaṁ kulaṁ me bhavanaṁ ca janma
tvayy āgate deva kulāni sapta
mātuḥ pituḥ sapta tathā priyayā
vaikuṇṭhalokaṁ gatavanti manye 5.6.3

Śrī Sudāmā said: “O Lord, now that you have come my family, my home, and my birth in this world have all become blessed. I think seven generations of ancestors in the families of my father, mother, and wife have now gone to Vaikuṇṭhaloka.”

bhū-bhāram āhartum alaṁ yadoḥ kule
jātau yuvāṁ pūrṇatamau pareśvarau
namo yuvābhyāṁ mama dīna-dīnaṁ
gṛhaṁ gatābhyāṁ jagad-īśvarau parau 5.6.4

“You are both the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead, who have taken birth in King Yadu’s dynasty to remove the earth’s burden. I offer my respectful obeisances to You, the two controllers of all the worlds, who have now come to my very poor home.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ity uktvā puṣpa-racanā-
laṅkārān madhupa-dhvanīn
nivedya makarandāṁś ca
mālā-kāro nanāma ha 5.6.5

Śrī Nārada said: “After speaking these words, the florist offered Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma many nectar flower ornaments filled with the humming of bees. Then he bowed down to offer his respects.”

dhṛtvā tat puṣpa-nicayaṁ
sa-balo bhagavān hariḥ
dattvā gopebhya ārāt taṁ
prāha prahasitānanaḥ 5.6.6

“Seeing that great abundance of flowers, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, gave them to the gopas. Smiling, He spoke the following words:”

garīyasī mat-pādābje
bhaktir bhūyāt sadā tava
mad-bhaktānāṁ tu saṅgaḥ syān
mat-svarūpam ihaiva hi 5.6.7

“You will have great devotion for My lotus feet. You will have the company of My devotees. Even in this world you will have a transcendental form like My own.”

baladevo dadau tasmai
śriyaṁ cānvaya-vardhinīm
utthāya tau tato rājann
anyaṁ vithiṁ prajagmatuḥ 5.6.8

“Then Lord Balarāma gave him the gift of eternally increasing handsomeness and opulence. O king, then Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma stood up and left. Now They traveled on a different road.”

yāntīṁ śriyaṁ padma-netraṁ
pāṭīrālepa-bhājanam
bibhratīṁ yuvatīṁ kubjāṁ
pathi papraccha mādhavaḥ 5.6.9

“Then Lord Kṛṣṇa posed a question to a young, beautiful, and lotus-eyed hunchback girl carrying a cup of sandal-paste as she walked on the road.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
kā tvaṁ kasya priyā su-bhru
kasyārthaṁ candanaṁ tv idam
dehy āvayor yena tava
ciraṁ śreyo bhaviṣyati 5.6.10

The Blessed Lord said: “Who are you? Who is the man that loves you? For whom do you carry this sandal paste? Give it to Us. If you do, you will attain eternal good fortune.”

śrī-sairandhry uvāca
dāsy asmi sundara-vara
kubjā nāma mahā-mate
mad-dhastotthaṁ ca pāṭīraṁ
jātaṁ bhoja-pateḥ priyam 5.6.11

The maidservant said: “O most handsome of men, I am a maidservant. O noble-hearted one, My name is Kubjā. I made this sandal paste with my own hand. Kaṁsa, the king of the Bhojas, likes it very much.”

adyāpi kaṁsa-dāsy asmi
samprāptaṁ tava cāgrataḥ
hasti-ṣuṇḍa-daṇḍa-same
bhuja-daṇḍe ’sti me manaḥ 5.6.12

“I am Kaṁsa’s maidservant, but now that I have met You I can think only of Your two broad arms like two great elephant’s trunks.”

yuvāṁ vinā ko ’nyatamo
’nulepaṁ kartum arhati
yuvayos tu samaṁ rūpaṁ
trailokye na hi vidyate 5.6.13

“Who deserves this sandal paste more than You two? In the three worlds no one is as handsome as You two.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
ubhābhyāṁ sa dadau sāndraṁ
harṣitā hy anulepanam
atha tāv aṅgarāgena
rāma-kṛṣṇau virejatuḥ 5.6.14

Śrī Nārada said: “Very happy, she gave the thick sandal paste to Them. Anointed with it, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma shone with great splendor.”

jagṛhuś candanaṁ divyaṁ
kiñcit kiñcid vrajārbhakāḥ
tri-vakrām atha tāṁ kṛṣṇo
ṛjvīṁ kartuṁ mano dadhe 5.6.15

“Then the boys of Vraja also took some of the splendid sandal paste. At that moment Lord Kṛṣṇa decided to transform the hunchback girl bent in three places into a beautiful straight girl.”

ākramya padbhyāṁ prapade ’ṅguli-dvayaṁ
prottāna-hastena vibhuḥ pareśvaraḥ
pragṛhya nṛṇāṁ cibuke prapaśyatāṁ
vakrāṁ tanuṁ tām udanīnamad dhariḥ 5.6.16

“With both feet stepping on her toes, and with two fingers of an outretched hand grasping her chin, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the almighty Supreme Personality of Godhead, as everyone watched, suddenly jerked her crooked body.”

tadaiva sā yaṣṭi-samāna-vigrahā
dīptyā ca rambhaṁ kṣipatīva rūpiṇī
bhūtvā gṛhītvāha hariṁ tu vāsasi
śuci-smitā jāta-manoja-vihvalā 5.6.17

“She suddenly became a beautiful girl straight as a stick, her straightness eclipsing the graceful straightness of the banana tree. Sweetly smiling, and now tormented with amorous desires, she clutched Lord Kṛṣṇa’s garment.”

śrī-sairandhry uvāca
gacchāśu he sundara-varya mad-gṛhaṁ
tyaktuṁ bhavantaṁ kila notsahe ’ham
prasīda sarvajña rasajña mānada
tvayā bhṛśaṁ pronmathitaṁ mano mama 5.6.18

The maidservant said: “O most handsome of men, please come to my house. I cannot leave You. O all-knowing one, O You who know the sweet taste of nectar, O You who humbly give all honor to others, please be kind to me. My heart is tormented with love for You.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tadaiva gopā jahasuḥ parasparaṁ
aho kim etat kara-tāla-niḥsvanaiḥ
prahasya rāmasya hariḥ prapaśyatas
tad yācyamāno hy avadat paraṁ vacaḥ 5.6.19

Śrī Nārada said: “Then the gopas burst into laughter and clapped their hands. Lord Kṛṣṇa smiled and, as Balarāma looked on, spoke the following words.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
aho ’ti-dhanyā mathurā purīyaṁ
vasanti yatraiva janās tu saumyāḥ
ye ’jñāta-panthān sva-gṛhaṁ nayanti
dṛṣṭvā purīṁ dhāma tavāgamiṣye 5.6.20

The Blessed Lord said: “Very beautiful and kind people, who take even strangers to be guests at their homes, live here. That is why Mathurā City is blessed with such opulences. After I have finished sightseeing in the city I will surely visit your home.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evam uktvottarīyāntaṁ
samākṛṣya girārdrayā
rāja-mārgaṁ vrajan kṛṣṇo
vaiśyān āḍhyān dadarśa ha 5.6.21

Śrī Nārada said: “After speaking these affectionate words, Lord Kṛṣṇa pulled at His upper garment and continued on the king’s road. Then He saw a group of wealthy vaiśyas.”

puṣpa-tāmbūla-gandhāḍhyaiḥ
phalair dugdha-phalair harim
sampūjya svāsane sthāpya
nemur āgrya-dhiyo viśaḥ 5.6.22

“Worshiping Him with flowers, betenuts, scents, fruits, and milk, and placing Him on a throne, the vaiśyas bowed down before Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

śrī-vaiśyā ūcuḥ
bhavec ced atra te rājyaṁ
tāvakān smaratāt tadā
vayaṁ tava prajā deva
rājye prāpte na kaḥ smaret 5.6.23

The vaiśyas said: “If You become the king here, then please remember Your own people. We are Your subjects. O Lord, who, on attaining a kingdom, will forget his own people?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
papraccha su-smito vaiśyān
ko-daṇḍa-sthānam acyutaḥ
na te tam ūcuḥ su-dhiyaḥ
ko-daṇḍe bhaṅga-śaṅkayā 5.6.24

Śrī Nārada said: “Sweetly smiling, the Lord asked where the great bow was kept. Fearing the Lord would break it, the intelligent vaiśyas did not tell Him.”

tad-rūpa-guṇa-mādhurya-
mohitā ye ca māthurāḥ
kumāra paśyaihi dhanur
ity ūcus tad-didṛkṣavaḥ 5.6.25

“Charmed by the Lord’s handsomeness, virtue and sweetness, and hoping to see the Lord break the bow, some boys of Mathurā said, “Child, come. Look. Here is the bow.”

tair dṛṣṭetena pathā kṛṣṇaḥ
praviṣṭo dhanuṣaḥ sthalam
maitrīṁ kurvan vayasyaiś ca
māthuraiḥ pura-bālakaiḥ 5.6.26

“At once becoming friends with these boys, the Lord followed their directions and came to the place of the bow.”

yathaindraṁ hema-citrādhyaṁ
ko-daṇḍaṁ sapta-tālakam
puruṣaiḥ pañca-sāhasrair
netuṁ yogyaṁ bṛhad-bharam 5.6.27

“Seven tālas long and wonderful with gold, the bow was like the bow of Indra himself. It was so heavy five thousand men were needed to carry it.”

aṣṭa-dhātu-mayaṁ kliṣṭaṁ
lakṣa-bhāra-samaṁ param
caturdaśyāṁ paura-janair
arcitaṁ yajña-maṇḍape 5.6.28

“It was made of eight metals and weighed a hundred thousand bhāras. On caturdaśī the people of the city worshiped it is the yajña-maṇḍapa.”

bhārgavena purā dattaṁ
yadu-rājāya mādhavaḥ
dadarśa kuṇḍalī-bhūtaṁ
sākṣāc cheṣam iva sthitam 5.6.29

“Long ago Lord Paraśurāma gave this bow to King Kaṁsa. Lord Kṛṣṇa looked at the bow and thought it was like Lord Śeṣa, His coils wound around and around.”

varyamāno nṛbhiḥ kṛṣṇaḥ
prasahya dhanur ādade
paśyatāṁ tatra paurāṇāṁ
sajjaṁ kṛtvātha līlayā 5.6.30

“Overcoming the people’s attempt to stop Him, Lord Kṛṣṇa picked up the bow. As the people of the city watched, He easily strung it.”

ākṛṣya karṇa-paryantaṁ
dor-daṇḍābhyāṁ harir dhanuḥ
babhañja madhyato rājann
ikṣu-daṇḍaṁ gajo yathā 5.6.31

“Grasbing the bow with both hands, and drawing the string to His ears, Lord Kṛṣṇa broke the bow in the middle, as an elephant breaks a sugarcane stalk.”

bhajyamānasya dhanuṣas
ṭāṅkāro ’bhūt taḍit-svanaḥ
nanāda tena brahmāṇḍaṁ
sapta-lokair bilaiḥ saha 5.6.32

“The sound of the bow breaking was like thunder. It echoed in the universe of seven lokas.”

vicelur dig-gajās tārā
rājad-bhū-khaṇḍa-maṇḍalam
tadaiva badhirī-bhūta
pṛthivyāṁ jana-maṇḍalī 5.6.33

“The earth, the stars, and the elephants holding up the directions all shook. The people on the earth were deafened by the sound.”

kaṁsasya hṛdayaṁ śabdo
vidadāra ghaṭī-dvayam
tad-rakṣiṇaḥ prakupitā
utthitā ātatāyinaḥ 5.6.34

“That sound shattered Kaṁsa’s heart. For two hours the guards at that place attacked Kṛṣṇa.”

gṛhītu-kāmāḥ śrī-kṛṣṇaṁ
pratyūcur badhyatām iti
atha tān āgatān vīkṣya
sa-śastrān bala-keśavau
ko-daṇḍa-sakale nītvā
jaghnatur durmadān bhṛśam
sakalāti-prahāreṇa
kecid vīras tu mūrchitāḥ 5.6.35-36

“Eager to grab Kṛṣṇa, the demon soldiers called out, Tie Him up!” Seeing them coming with weapons, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma took up the bow and struck them so ferociously than many fell unconscious.”

bhinna-pādā bhinna-nakhāḥ
kecic chinnāṁsa-bāhavaḥ
vīrāḥ pañca-sahasrāṇi
nipetur bhūmi-maṇḍale 5.6.37

“Their feet, nails, arms, or shoulders broken, five thousand soldiers fell to the ground.”

vicelur māthurāḥ sarve
dudruvus tad-didṛkṣavaḥ
puryāṁ kolāhale jāte
nṛṇāṁ jātaṁ mahad bhayam 5.6.38

“Trembling, the people ran to see it. There was a great uproar in the city. The people were very afraid.”

bhoja-rāja-sabhā-cchatraṁ
akasmān nipapāta ha
gopālaiḥ sa-balaḥ kṛṣṇo
dhāvan cāpa-sthalān nṛpa āyayau nanda-nikaṭe sandhyā-kāle ’ti-bhīta-vat 5.6.39

“At that moment the royal parasol over Kaṁsa’s throne suddenly fell to the ground. O king, Kṛṣṇa and the gopas fled from the place of the bow. Finding Nanda at sunset, Lord Kṛṣṇa seemed to be very afraid.”

nirīkṣya govinda-su-rūpam adbhutaṁ
vimohitā vai mathurā-purāṅgaṇaḥ
visraṣṭa-vāsaḥ-kabarāḥ smarādhayaḥ
parasparaṁ prāhur idaṁ sakhī-janam 5.6.40

“Gazing at Lord Kṛṣṇa’s wonderfully handsome form, the women of Mathurā were bewildered with feelings of love. Tormented with amorous desires, and their hair and garments in disarray, they spoke among themselves.”

śrī-purandhrya ūcuḥ
kandarpa-koṭi-dyutim āharaṁs tvaraṁ
svairaṁ caran vai mathurā-pure hariḥ
nirīkṣyate yābhir atīva sākṣād
aṅgeṣu sarveṣv api naḥ samāviśat 5.6.41

The women of Mathurā City said: “As we gaze on Lord Kṛṣṇa, eclipsing the glory of millions of Kāmadevas and wandering as He likes in Mathurā City, amorous desires esuddenly enter all limbs of our bodies.”

śrī-kuśalā ūcuḥ
krūrāḥ striyaḥ kiṁ na hi santi paṭṭane
nirīkṣyate yābhir anaṅga-mohitāḥ
aṅgeṣu sarveṣv api sarva-sundaro
nāsmābhir ānandamayo nirīkṣyate 5.6.43 kasyaika-deśe madhuratvam īkṣyate
tatrāsti netraṁ prapatat-pataṅgavat
yas tv eva sarvāṅga-mānoharaḥ sakhi
sa eva netreṇa kathaṁ samīkṣyate 5.6.43

“How can there be so much sweetness in a single place? Gazing at Him, our eyes have become like moths flying into a fire. O friend, how do my eyes have the power to see someone so handsome?”

aṅge hy aṅge sundare nanda-sūnoḥ
prāptaṁ prāptaṁ yatra yatrāpi netram
tasmāt tasmān nāmaval labdha-saukhyaṁ
lāvaṇyābdhau magnaval lagna-cittam 5.6.44

“As our eyes gaze on each of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s limbs we become filled with bliss and our thoughts become plunged in the ocean of His handsomess.”

Chapter 7 - The Killing of Kuvalayāpīḍa

śrī-nārada uvāca
rajakasya śiraś-chedaṁ
kaṁso vai rakṣiṇāṁ vadham
dhanur-bhaṅgaṁ tataḥ śrutvā
paraṁ trāsam upāgamat 5.7.1

Śrī Nārada said: “When he heard how the washerman was beheaded, the guards killed, and the bow broken, Kaṁsa became filled with fear.”

tat-kṣaṇād dur-nimittāni
vāmāṅga-sphuraṇāni ca
prapaśyann aṅga-bhaṅgāni
na nidrāṁ prāpa daitya-rāṭ 5.7.2

“From that moment Kaṁsa saw many evil omens. The left side of his body trembled. In dreams he saw his limbs broken. Then he could not sleep.”

svapne pretaiḥ samāyuktas
tailābhyakto dig-ambaraḥ
japā-srāṅ mahisārūḍho
dakṣiṇāśāṁ jagāma saḥ 5.7.3

“Then he dreamed of being attacked by ghosts, of being anointed with oil, of walking about naked, of being garlanded with roses, and of rising south on a buffalo.”

prātaḥ-kāle samutthāya
kārya-bhāra-karān janān
āhūya kārayām āsa
malla-krīḍā-mahotsavam 5.7.4

“Rising in the morning, he called for his servants and had them make arrangements for the day’s wrestling festival.”

viśālājira-samyukte
hema-stambha-samanvite
sabhā-maṇḍapa-deśāgre
raṅga-bhūmir babhūva ha 5.7.5

“Before the royal assembly building was a sports arena with a huge field and many golden pillars.”

vitānair hema-saṅkāśair
muktā-dāma-vilambibhiḥ
sopānair hema-mañcaiś ca
raṅga-bhūmir babhau nṛpa 5.7.6

“Om king, there were golden awnings decorated with pearls, great stairways, golden viewer’s boxes, and a great field.”

rāja-mañce ratna-maye
makarandārcite śubhe
śakra-siṁhāsanaṁ tatra
sopabarhaṇa-maṇḍalam 5.7.7

“In the splendid, golden, jamine-decorated royal box was Indra’s throne holding a great circle of cushions.”

ātapatreṇa divyena
candra-maṇḍala-cāruṇā
haṁsābhair vyajanair yuktais
cāmarair vajra-muṣṭibhiḥ 5.7.8

“Over the throne was a splendid parasol graceful as the circle of the moon, and by it were swan fans and cāmaras with diamond handles.”

daśa-hastocchritaṁ saśvad
viśvakarma-vinirmitam
tadāruhya babhau kaṁso
’dri-śṛṅge mṛgarāḍ iva 5.7.9

“It was ten hastas high and was made by Viśvakarmā. At that moment Kaṁsa ascended the throne. He was like a lion on a mountaintop. Note: One hasta is the distance between the elbow and the middle finger.”

gāyakaḥ prajagus tatra
nanṛtur vāra-yoṣitaḥ
nedur mṛdaṅga-paṭaha-
tāla-bherya-nakādayaḥ 5.7.10

“Suddenly the singers sang, the dancing-girls danced, and the mṛdaṅgas, paṭahas, tālas, bheryas, nakas, and other musical instruments were sounded.”

rājāno maṇḍaleśāś ca
paurā janapadā nṛpa
dadṛśur malla-yuddhaṁ te
mañce mañce samāsthitāḥ 5.7.11

“In the many grandstands and viwers’ boxes many kings, provincial governors, and the people of the city, observed the wrestling games.”

cāṇūro muṣṭikaḥ kūṭaḥ
śalas tośala eva ca
vyāyāma-mudgarair yuktā
yuyudhus te paraspaaram 5.7.12

“Then Cāṇūra, Muṣṭika, Kūṭa, Śala, and Tośala entered and began to fight each other with clubs.”

nanda-rājādayo gopāḥ
kaṁsāhūtā natānanāḥ
dattvā baliṁ paraṁ tasmā
ekasmin mañca āśritāḥ 5.7.13

“Called forward by Kaṁsa, King Nanda and the other gopas bowed their heads and placed their gifts before him. Then they went to their own box.”

bāṇāsura-jarāsandha-
narakānāṁ purān nṛpa
anyeṣāṁ śambarādīnāṁ
sakāśād bhūbhujāṁ tathā
balayaś cāyayū rājan
yadu-rājāya tatra vai
atha tau rāma-kṛṣṇau dvau
māyā-bālaka-vigrahau
malla-līlā-darśanārthaṁ yayatū raṅga-maṇḍapam 5.7.14-15

“Many offerings also came to Kaṁsa from Bāṇāsura, Jarāsandha, Naraka, Śambara, and many other kings. Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, who seemed to be ordinary human boys, also approached the arena to see the wrestling games.”

gomūtra-caya-sindūra-
kastūrī-patra-bhṛn-mukhaṁ sravan-mada-mahā-mattaṁ ratna-kuṇḍala-
maṇḍitam
gajaṁ kuvalayāpīḍaṁ
raṅga-dvāram avasthitam
vīkṣya kṛṣṇo mahā-mātraṁ
prāha gambhīrayā girā 5.7.16-17

“Seeing the maddened elephant Kuvalayāpīḍa anointed with gomūtra, sindūra, and musk, decorated with jewel earrings, and its ichor flowing, blocking the entrance to the arena, in a deep voice Lord Kṛṣṇa said to the elephant trainer:”

ākarṣayāṅga nāgendraṁ
mārgaṁ kuru mamecchayā
no cet tvāṁ pātayiṣyāmi
sa-nāgaṁ bhūmi-maṇḍale 5.7.18

“Sir, please pull back this great elephant, and give Me a path as I wish. If you do not, I will throw you and your elephant to the ground.”

mahā-mātras tadā kruddho
nodayām āsa taṁ gajam
cītkāram utkaṭaṁ dikṣu
kurvantaṁ nanda-sūnave 5.7.19

“Angered, the elephant-trainer made the elephant, trumpeting in all directions, charge Lord Kṛṣṇa, the son of Nanda.”

gṛhītvā taṁ hariṁ sadyaḥ
śuṇḍā-daṇḍena nāga-rāṭ
ujjahāra tatas tasmān
nirgato bhāra-bhṛd dhariḥ 5.7.20

“The regal elephant grabbed Kṛṣṇa in its trunk and lifted Him high, but in a moment Kṛṣṇa slipped from the elephant’s grasp.”

tat-pādeṣu vilīno ’bhūt
prabhraman sann itas tataḥ
vṛndāvana-nikuñjeṣu
vṛkṣeṣu ca yathā hariḥ 5.7.21

“As He used to do among the trees in Vṛndāvana’s forests, Kṛṣṇa, quickly going here and there, played hide-and-seek behind the elephant’s legs.”

kare jagrāha taṁ nāgaḥ
śuṇḍā-daṇḍena cāṅghriṣu
niṣpīḍya śuṇḍāṁ haṣṭābhyāṁ
hariḥ paścād vinirgataḥ 5.7.22

“Pushing its trunk between its legs, the elephant grabbed Kṛṣṇa. Pressing the trunk hard with both hands, Kṛṣṇa ran behind the elephant.”

tiryag-bhūtaś ca taṁ nāgo
gṛhītum upacakrame
muṣṭinā taṁ ghātayitvā
puro dudrāva mādhavaḥ 5.7.23

“Its trunk and body twisted into an awkward position, the disoriented elephant tried to grab Kṛṣṇa, but Kṛṣṇa punched it with His fist and ran to the front.”

tam anvadhāvan nāgendro
mathurāyāṁ videha-rāṭ
kolāhale tadā jāte
haris tasmād ito yayau 5.7.24

“O king of Videha, when Kṛṣṇa fled and the elephant chased Him through Mathurā City, there was great uproar.”

pucche gṛhītvā taṁ nāgaṁ
baladevo mahā-balaḥ
cakarṣa bhuja-daṇḍābhyāṁ
phaṇināṁ garuḍo yathā 5.7.25

“Then very powerful Lord Balarāma grabbed the elephant’s tail and with both arms dragged it here and there as Garuḍa drags a snake.”

prahasan bhagavān kṛṣṇo
gṛhītvā taṁ kare balāt
cakarṣa bhuja-daṇḍābhyāṁ
kūpa-rajjuṁ yathā naraḥ 5.7.26

“Laughing, Lord Kṛṣṇa grabbed the elephant’s trunk and pulled it with both hands as one pulls the rope in a well.”

dvayor ākarṣanān nāgo
vihvalo ’bhūn nṛpeśvara
mahā-mātrās tadā sapta
ruruhus taṁ gajaṁ balāt 5.7.27

“O king of kings, pulled in both directions by Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, the elephant was in great distress. Seven elephant trainers quickly came to its rescue.”

nītā gajās tathā cānyaiḥ
kṛṣṇaṁ hantuṁ śata-trayam
aṅkuśāsphālanāt kruddhaṁ
mattebhaṁ punar āgatam śrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān sākṣād baladevasya paśyataḥ
śuṇḍā-daṇḍe saṅgṛhītvā
bhrāmayitvā tv itas tataḥ
pātayām āsa bhū-pṛṣṭhe
kamaṇḍalum ivārbhakaḥ 5.7.28-29

“Then other trainers brought three hundred elephants to kill Lord Kṛṣṇa. As Lord Balarāma watched, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, approached the elephant maddened by the pinching goad, grabbed it by the trunk, whirled around and around, and finally threw it to the ground as a small child throws a kamaṇḍalu.”

dūre prapatitās tasya
mahā-mātra itas tataḥ
satāṁ prapaśyatāṁ nāgaḥ
sadyo vai nidhanaṁ gataḥ 5.7.31

“As the saintly people of Mathurā watched and the trainer fled, the elephant suddenly died.”

taj-jyotiḥ śrī-ghana-śyāme
līnaṁ jātaṁ videha-rāṭ
dantāv utpatya tasyāpi
rāma-kṛṣṇau mahā-balau nijaghnatur mahā-mātrān mṛgān keśariṇau yathā 5.7.32

“O king of Videha, an effulgence left the elephant’s dead body and suddenly entered Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is dark as a monsoon cloud. Then very powerful Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma uprooted the elaphant’s tusks and with them as weapons attacked the elephant-trainers as two lions attack a flock of deer.”

dvipe hate ’pi ye cānye
mahā-mātra itas tataḥ
vidudruvur yathā meghā
varṣa-kāle gate sati 5.7.33

“When the elephant was killed, the trainers fled in all directions as rainclouds flee at the end of the monsoon.”

evaṁ hatvā dvipaṁ gopaiḥ
śeṣais taiḥ prekṣanotsukaiḥ
jayārāvai rāma-kṛṣṇau
śrama-vāri-madāṅkitau
pariśramāruṇa-mukhau
raṅgaṁ viviśatur tvaram
danta-pāṇī mahā-vegau
yathāśām anilānalau 5.7.34-35

“After killing the elephant, very powerful Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, covered with perspiration and elephant’s rut, clutching the two elephant’s tusks, and Their faces red with stress, quickly entered the arena like two forest-fires fanned by great winds. Following Them were the remaining gopas, who loudly called out “Victory!” and were eager to see what would happen next.”

mallāś ca mallaṁ ca narā narendraṁ
striyaḥ smaraṁ gopa-gaṇā vrajeśam
pitā sutaṁ daṇḍa-dharaṁ hy asanto
mṛtyuṁ ca kaṁso vibudhā virājam
tattvaṁ paraṁ yogi-varāś ca bhojā
devaṁ tadā raṅga-gataṁ balena
pṛthak pṛthag bhāvanayā hy apaśyan
sarve janās taṁ paripūrṇa-devam 5.7.36-37

“When, accompanied by Balarāma, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, entered the arena, the people saw Him in different ways. The wrestlers saw Him as a wrestler, the people in general saw Him as a great king, the women saw Him as Kāmadeva, the gopas saw Him as the master of Vraja, King Nanda saw Him as his son, the impious saw Him as Yamarāja, Kaṁsa saw Him as death personified, the wise saw Him as a great renounced sage, the yogīs saw Him as the Supreme Truth, and the members of the Bhoja dynasty saw Him as their Lord.”

hataṁ dvipaṁ vīkṣya ca tau mahā-balau
kaṁso manasvī bhayam āpa cetasi
mañca-sthitā harṣita-mānasaś ca
candraṁ cakora iva te sukhaṁ yayuḥ 5.7.38

“When Kaṁsa saw that the elephant had been killed, he became pensive and frightened at heart. When the people in the grandstands saw it, they became happy at heart like cakora birds gazing at the moon.”

karṇe ca karṇaṁ vinidhāya nāgarā
mahotsukās te hy avadan parasparam
etau ki sākṣāt parameśvarau parau
babhūvatur vai vasudeva-nandanau 5.7.39

“The eager people whispered in each other’s ears, The Supreme Personality of Godhead has become the two sons of Vasudeva.”

aho ’ti-ramyaṁ vraja-maṇḍalaṁ paraṁ
yatraiṣa sākṣād vicacāra mādhavaḥ
kṛtvā hi yad-darśanam adya durlabhaṁ
vayaṁ kṛtārthās tu bhavema sarvataḥ 5.7.40

“We are now gazing on Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who wandered in the beautiful circle of Vraja, and who is so difficult to see. Now our lives have become a perfect success.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
vadatsu paura-lokeṣu
nadat-tūryeṣu maithila
cāṇūras tāv upavrajya
rāma-kṛṣṇāv uvāca ha 5.7.41

Śrī Nārada said: “O king of Mithilā, as the people of Mathurā spoke in this way, and as the turyas sounded, Cāṇūra approached Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma and addressed Them with these words.”

cāṇūra uvāca
he rāma he kṛṣṇa yuvāṁ mahā-balau
rājñaḥ puro vai kurutaṁ mṛdhaṁ balāt
praharṣite rājani ced yadūttame
kiṁ kiṁ na bhadraṁ bhavatīha vaś ca naḥ 5.7.42

Cāṇūra said: “O Balarāma, O Kṛṣṇa, You are both very strong. Please fight with us before the king. If King Kaṁsa is pleased what good thing will not come both to You and to us.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
puraiva bhadraṁ nṛpateḥ prasādato
bālā vayaṁ tulya-balaiś ca bālakaiḥ
bhūyān mṛdho no balavān yathocitaṁ
adharma-yuddhaṁ kila mā bhaved iha 5.7.43

The Blessed Lord said: “By the king’s grace we will happily fight with other boys that are our equals in strength. It is not right for Us to fight with those who are much stronger than We. There should not be such an impious fight.”

cāṇūra uvāca
bhavān na bālo na ca vā kiśoro
balaś ca sākṣād balināṁ balīyān
sahasra-mattebha-balaṁ dadhāno
dvipo bhavadbhyāṁ nihataḥ sa-līlam 5.7.44

Cāṇūra said: “You are not a boy. Nor are You a youth. You are stronger than the strongest. The two of You easily killed an elephant stronger than thousands of furious elephants.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ tasya vacaḥ śrutvā
bhagavān vṛjinārdanaḥ
cāṇūreṇāpi yuyudhe
muṣṭikena balo balī 5.7.45

Śrī Nārada said: “Hearing these words, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who removes all troubles, proceeded to fight with Cāṇūra. Then Balarāma proceeded to fight with Muṣṭika.”

ākarṣaṇaṁ nodanaṁ ca
bhujābhyāṁ bhuja-daṇḍayoḥ
cakratuḥ paśyatāṁ nṛṇāṁ
gajāv iva jigīṣayā 5.7.46

“As the people watched, Kṛṣṇa and Cāṇura wrestled, pulling and pushing each other with both arms. They were like two elephants trying to defeat each other.”

hastābhyāṁ vapur utthāpya
cāṇūrasya hariḥ svayam
atolayad deha-bhāraṁ
puṇya-bhāraṁ yathā vidhiḥ 5.7.47

“Picking him up with both arms, Kṛṣṇa weighed Cāṇūra, as the demigod Brahma weighs the heaviness of someone’s pious deeds.”

cāṇūras taṁ hariṁ devaṁ
kareṇaikena līlayā
ujjahāra mahā-vīro
bhū-khaṇḍaṁ nāga-rāḍ iva 5.7.48

“As Lord Śeṣa, the king of serpents, picks up the earth, with one hand Cāṇūra playfully picked up Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

grīvāyāṁ kila cāṇūraṁ
bhuja-vegena mādhavaḥ
kaṭyāṁ coddhṛtya sahasā
pātayām āsa bhū-tale 5.7.49

“With one hand on his neck and the other on his waist, Kṛṣṇa suddenly picked up Cāṇūra and threw him to the gound.”

hastaiś ca jānubhiḥ pādair
bhujair aṅguli-muṣṭibhiḥ
jaghnatuḥ kṛṣṇa-cāṇūrau
tathaiva bala-muṣṭikau 5.7.50

“Hitting each other with Their hands, knees, feet, arms, fingers, and fists, Kṛṣṇa and Cāṇūra fought. Balarāma and Muṣṭika also fought in the same way.”

śrama-vāri-yute dṛṣṭvā
śrī-mukhe rāma-kṛṣṇayoḥ
sānukampās tadā prāhur
gavākṣa-sthāḥ nṛpa-striyaḥ 5.7.51

“Gazing at the handsome perspiring faces of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, the women of the palace became filled with compassion for Them. Standing in the palace windows, they spoke.”

śrī-striya ūcuḥ
aho adharmaḥ su-mahat-sabhāyāṁ
jātaḥ puro rājani vartamāne
kva vajra-tulyāṅga-vṛtau hi mallau
kva puṣpa-tulyau bata rāma-kṛṣṇau 5.7.52

The women said: “An impious fight is now happening in the royal assembly in the king’s presence. How can Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, who are like two flowers, be an equal match with these two wrestlers, who have bodies like thunderbolts?”

aho hy abhāgyaṁ hi puraukasāṁ no
yuddhe tayor darśanam adya jātam
aho ’ti-dhanyaṁ bata bhūri-bhāgyaṁ
vanaukasāṁ rāsa-rasena jātam 5.7.53

“The people of Mathurā City are very unfortunate that they must see such a fight. The girls in the forest were fortunate. They could taste the nectar of the rāsa-dance with Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

Chapter 8 - The Killing of Kaṃsa

śrī-nārada uvāca
ardra-cittaṁ nanda-rājaṁ
vanitānāṁ manoratham
smṛtvā śatrūn hantu-kāmas
cakre yuddhaṁ balād dhariḥ 5.8.1

Śrī Nārada said: “Thinking of how King Nanda’s heart was melting and how the women were praying in this way, Lord Kṛṣṇa, yearning to kill them, ferociously attacked His opponents.”

gṛhītvā bhuja-daṇḍābhyāṁ
cāṇūraṁ gagane balāt
cikṣepa sahasā kṛṣṇo
vātaḥ padmam ivoddhṛtam 5.8.2

“Suddenly grabbing him with both hands, Lord Kṛṣṇa threw Cāṇura into the sky, as a great wind uproots and throws a lotus flower.”

ākāśāt patitaḥ so ’pi
tārakena hy adho-mukhaḥ
utthāya muṣṭinā kṛṣṇaṁ
tāḍayām āsa vegataḥ 5.8.3

“Like a comet Caṇūra fell headfirst from the sky. Standing up, he violently punched Kṛṣṇa.”

tasya muṣṭi-prahāreṇa
na cacāla parāt paraḥ
sadyo gṛhītvā cāṇūraṁ
pātayām āsa bhū-tale 5.8.4

“Unmoved by the punch, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is greater than the greatest, suddenly grabbed Cāṇūra and threw him to the ground.”

bhinna-dantas tu cāṇūraḥ
krodha-yukto madotkaṭaḥ
muṣṭi-dvayena śrī-kṛṣṇaṁ
tatāḍa hṛdi maithila 5.8.5

“O king of Mithilā, mad with anger, and his teeth now broken, with both fists Cāṇūra pounded Lord Kṛṣṇa’s chest.”

gṛhītvā karayos taṁ vai
karābhyāṁ bhagavān svayaṁ
kaṁsasyāgre bhrāmayitvā
sarveṣāṁ paśyatāṁ nṛpa 5.8.6

“O king, grabbing both his hands, Lord Kṛṣṇa whirled Cānūra around and around as Kaṁsa and everyone else looked on.”

pātayām āsa bhū-pṛṣṭhe
kamaṇḍalum ivārbhakaḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇasya prahāreṇa
cāṇūro bhinna-mastakaḥ
udvaman rudhiraṁ rājan
sadyo vai nidhanaṁ gataḥ 5.8.7

“As a child throws a kamaṇḍalu, Lord Kṛṣṇa threw Cāṇūra to the ground. O king, vomiting blood, and his head broken, Cāṇūra suddenly died.”

tathaiva muṣṭikaṁ mallaṁ
muṣṭibhir yudhi durgamam
dhṛtvāṅghrau bhrāmayitvā khe
baladevo mahā-balaḥ
pātayām āsa bhū-pṛṣṭhe
phaṇinaṁ garuḍo yathā muṣṭiko nidhanaṁ prāpa prodvaman rudhiraṁ mukhāt 5.8.8-9

“In the same way powerful Lord Balarāma grabbed the feet of invincible Muṣṭika, whirled him around, and threw him to the ground as Garuḍa throws a snake. O king, vomiting blood from his mouth, Muṣṭika suddenly died.”

kūṭaṁ samāgataṁ vīkṣya
baladevo mahā-balaḥ
muṣṭinā pātayām āsa
vajreṇendro yathā girim 5.8.10

“Seeing Kūṭa coming, with a single punch Lord Balarāma knocked him to the ground as with his thunderbolt Indra knocks down a mountain.”

prāptaṁ śālaṁ nanda-sūnur
lattayā taṁ tatāḍa ha
tīkṣnayā tuṇḍayā rājan
kadrujaṁ garuḍo yathā 5.8.11

“When Śala approached, Lord Kṛṣṇa attacked him as with his beak Garuḍa attacks a snake.”

gṛhītvā tośalaṁ kṛṣṇo
madhyataḥ samvidārya ca
prākṣipat kaṁsa-mañcāgre
viṭapaṁ sindhuro yathā 5.8.12

“Then Kṛṣṇa grabbed Tośala, ripped him in two at the waist, and threw the two pieces into Kaṁsa’s box as an elephant breaks and throws a tree.”

ete nipatitā raṅge
sadyo vai nidhanaṁ gatāḥ
teṣāṁ jyotiṁsi vaikuṇṭhe
viviśuḥ paśyatāṁ satām 5.8.13

“As the saintly people of Mathurā looked on, the effulgent souls of the wrestlers killed in the arena entered the spiritual world of Vaikuṇṭha.”

evaṁ śrī-rāma-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ
malleṣu nihateṣu ca
śeṣāḥ pradudruvur mallā
bhayārtā jīvanecchayā 5.8.14

“After many wrestlers were thus killed by Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, the others, terrified and intent on staying alive, fled.”

śrīdāmādīn vayasyāṁś ca
gopān ākṛṣya mādhavaḥ
taiḥ sārdhaṁ yuddham ārebhe
sarveṣāṁ paśyatāṁ satām 5.8.15

“Then, calling Śrīdāmā and His other gopa friends, Kṛṣṇa began to fight with them as the saintly people of Mathurā looked on.”

kirīṭa-kuṇḍala-dharau
rāma-kṛṣṇau sahārbhakaiḥ
viharantau vīkṣya raṅge
visismuḥ pura-vāsinaḥ 5.8.16

“As Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, wearing crowns and earrings, played with the gopa boys in the arena, the people of Mathurā gazed at Them and became filled with wonder.”

kaṁsaṁ vinā sarva-mukhāj
jaya-śabdo vinirgataḥ
sādhu sādhv iti vādo ’bhūn
nedur dundubhayas tataḥ 5.8.17

“From every mouth, except Kaṁsa’s, came the words Victory!” and “Bravo! Bravo!” Then all the dundubhi drums sounded.”

svasyājayaṁ vīkṣya kaṁso
mahā-krodha-samākulaḥ
varjayitvā tūrya-ghoṣaṁ
prāha prasphuritādharaḥ 5.8.18

“Seeing his own men defeated, Kaṁsa will filled with anger. Stopping the tūrya drums, he spoke, his lips trembling.”

kaṁsa uvāca
durbuddhi-yuktau vasudeva-nandanau
prasahya niḥsārayatāśu mat-purāt
harantu sarvaṁ vraja-vāsināṁ dhanaṁ
badhnīta nandaṁ sahasāti-durmatim 5.8.19

“Wicked-hearted Nanda should be bound and imprisoned.”

adyograsenasya pituḥ ku-buddheḥ
śaureḥ siraś cāśu hi chindhi chindhi
kau yatra tatrāpi tathātra vṛṣṇi-
jātān suraṁśān kila sūdayadhvam 5.8.20

“Ugrasena, my foolish father, and foolish Vasudeva should both be beheaded. Wherever they are on this earth, the Vṛṣṇis, who are incarnations of the demigods, should be killed.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ vikathamānasya
kaṁsasya yadu-nandanaḥ
sahasotpatya taṁ mañcaṁ
āruhat krodha-pūritaḥ 5.8.21

Śrī Nārada said: “As Kaṁsa was speaking in this wild way, Lord Kṛṣṇa, filled with anger, suddenly jumped into his box.”

mṛtyuṁ samāgataṁ vīkṣya
mañcād utthāya sa-tvaram
madoddhato bhartsayaṁs taṁ
jagṛhe khaḍga-carmaṇī 5.8.22

“Seeing that his death had come, Kaṁsa jumped from the box and, wildly rebuking Kṛṣṇa, grabbed a sword and shield.”

agrahīt sahasā kaṁsaṁ
dorbhyāṁ carmāsi-samyutam
yathā tuṇḍa-vibhāgābhyāṁ
sa-viṣaṁ phaṇināṁ virāṭ 5.8.23

“As Kaṁsa tried to defend himself with sword and shield, Lord Kṛṣṇa suddenly grabbed him with both arms as Garuḍa grabs a poisonous snake in his beak.”

patat-khaḍgaś calac-carmā
bhuja-bandhād balād balī
viniryayau tārkṣya-tuṇḍāt
puṇḍarīko yathā phaṇī 5.8.24

“Fighting with his sword and shield, Kaṁsa escaped Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arms as the snake Puṇḍarīka escaped Garuḍa’s beak.”

mañce tau balinau vegān
mardayantau parasparam
śaila-śṛṅge yathā siṁhau
suśubhāte yathā-tatham 5.8.25

“In the box again, the two strong opponents hit each other very hard. They were like two splendid lions fighting on a mountaintop.”

utpatantaṁ balāt kaṁsaṁ
śata-hastaṁ mahāmbare
agrahīc cotpatan kṛṣṇo
syenaṁ syeno yathāmbare 5.8.26

“When Kaṁsa jumped a hundred hastas into the sky, Lord Kṛṣṇa jumped after him and grabbed him. They both fought like two eagles in the sky.”

gṛhītvā bhuja-daṇḍābhyāṁ
pracaṇḍaṁ daitya-puṅgavam
trailokya-bala-dhṛg devo
bhrāmayitvā tv itas tataḥ 5.8.27

“Then Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the strongest person in the three worlds, with both hands grabbed the ferocious demon Kaṁsa and whirled him around and around.”

ākāśāt pātayām āsa
mañcopari ruṣānvitaḥ
bhagna-daṇḍo ’bhavan mañcas
taḍit-pāte yathā drumaḥ 5.8.28

“Angry Kṛṣṇa then threw him into the box at the arena. The box shattered like a tree struck by lightning.”

patito ’pi sa vajrāṅgaḥ
kiñcid vyākula-mānasaḥ
sahasotthāya yuyudhe
śrī-kṛṣṇena mahātmanā 5.8.29

“The Kaṁsa, his body hard as thunderbolts and his mind a little agitated by all this, jumped from the box and continued to fight with Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

nītvā taṁ bhuja-daṇḍābhyāṁ
mañce kṣiptvā punaḥ prabhuḥ
āruhya hṛdayaṁ tasya
mauliṁ jagrāha mādhavaḥ 5.8.30

“Grabbing him with both hands, throwing him again into the box, and knocking away his crown, Lord Kṛṣṇa straddled Kaṁsa’s chest.”

sadyaḥ pragṛhya keśeṣu
raṅgopari hariḥ svayam
mañcāt taṁ pātayām āsa
śailād gaṇḍa-śilām iva 5.8.31

“Then, grabbing his hair, Lord Kṛṣṇa threw Kaṁsa from the box into the arena as a thunderstorm throws a great boulder from a mountaintop.”

tasyopariṣṭhāc chrī-kṛṣṇaḥ
sarvādharaḥ sanātanaḥ
nipapāta svayaṁ vegād
ananto ’nanta-vikramaḥ 5.8.32

“The Lord Kṛṣṇa, the eternal, limitless, and unlimitedly powerful Supreme Personality of Godhead, who maintains everyone, jumped on the demon Kaṁsa.”

itthaṁ dvayor nipātena
nimnaṁ bhū-khaṇḍa-maṇḍalam
sthalīva sahasā rājan
cakampe ghaṭikā-dvayam 5.8.33

“As the two of them fought the earth cracked. O king, for two hours the earth shook.”

samparetaṁ bhoja-rājaṁ
bhūmau taṁ vicakarṣa ha
yathā mṛgendro nāgendraṁ
sarveṣāṁ paśyatāṁ nṛpa 5.8.34

“O king, as everyone looked on, Lord Kṛṣṇa dragged the dead Kaṁsa across the ground as a lion drags a great elephant.”

hā-hā-kāras tadaivāsīd
dhāvatāṁ bhubhujāṁ nṛpa
vaira-bhāvena deveśaṁ
bhajan kaṁso mahā-balaḥ
jagāma tasya sārūpyaṁ
bhṛṅginaḥ kīṭako yathā 5.8.35

“O king, the many kings ran here and there, exclaiming, Alas! Alas!” Then because in the rasa of hatred he had worshiped Lord Kṛṣṇa, the master of the demigods, Kaṁsa attained a spiritual form resembling the Lord’s, as a kiṭaka insect attains the form of it’s enemy, the bee.”

kaṁsaṁ prapatitaṁ dṛṣṭvā
bhrātaro ’ṣṭau mahā-balāḥ sunāma-sṛṣṭi-nyagrodha-tuṣṭimad-rāṣṭra-pālakāḥ
suhunā kaṅka-śaṅkubhyāṁ
krodha-prasphuritādharāḥ
khaḍga-carma-dharā yoddhuṁ
kṛṣṇopari samāyayuḥ 5.8.36-37

“Seeing that Kaṁsa had fallen, Kaṁsa’s eight brothers, Sṛṣṭi, Nyagrodha, Tuṣṭimān, Rāṣṭrapālaka, Suhu, Kaṅka, and Śaṅku, clutching swords and shields, and their lips trembling with rage, rushed Śrī Kṛṣṇa to fight with Him.”

vīkṣya tān mudgaraṁ nītvā
rohinī-nandano balaḥ
ārāc cakāra huṅkāraṁ
yathā siṁho mṛgān prati 5.8.38
