“Seeing them coming, Lord Balarāma took up a club and roared like a lion to herd of deer.”

huṅkāreṇaiva śastrāṇi
teṣāṁ hastebhya ābhayāt
petur amra-phalānīva
daṇḍa-ghātaiś ca maithila 5.8.39

“The brothers were terrified by that roar. Their weapons suddenly fell, as mangoes fall when hit by a stick.”

niḥśastrās te mahā-vīrā
muṣṭibhiḥ sarvato balam
teḍuḥ śailaṁ yathā nāgāḥ
śuṇḍā-daṇḍair itas tataḥ 5.8.40

“Now weaponless, the ferocious fighters attacked Lord Balarāma with their fists. They were like many elephants whipping a great mountain with their trunks.”

sṛṣṭiṁ tathā sunāmānaṁ
mudgareṇa balo ’hanat
nyagrodhaṁ bhuja-vegena
kaṅkaṁ vāma-kareṇa vai 5.8.41

“With the club Lord Balarāma killed Sṛṣṭi and Sunāma, with His arms He killed Nygrodha, and with His left hand He killed Kaṅka.”

śaṅkuṁ suhuṁ tuṣṭimantaṁ
vāma-pādena mādhavaḥ
rāṣṭrapālaṁ dakṣiṇena
pādenābhijaghāna ha 5.8.42

“With His left foot Lord Kṛṣṇa killed Śaṅku, Suhu, and Tuṣṭimān, and with His right foot He killed Rāṣṭrapālaka.”

aṣṭau nipetuḥ sahasā
vṛkṣā vāta-hatā iva
teṣāṁ jyotir bhagavati
līnaṁ jātaṁ videha-rāṭ 5.8.43

“The eight brothers quickly fell like tress struck by a great wind. O king of Videha, sparks of light left their corpses and merged in the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

deva-dundubhayo nedur
jaya-dhvanir abhūt tadā
sadyo vai vavṛṣur devāḥ
puṣpair nandana-sambhavaiḥ 5.8.44

“Suddenly the dundubhi drums of the demigods sounded and the demigods showered flowers from the Nandana gardens.”

vidyādharyaś ca gandharvyo
nanṛtur harṣa-vihvalāḥ
vidyādharāś ca gandharvāḥ
kinnarās tad-yaśo jaguḥ 5.8.45

“The Vidyādhara and Gandharva women danced as the Vidyādhara, Gandharva, and Kinnara men sang the glories of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.”

brahmādyā munayaḥ siddhā
vimānair draṣṭum āgatāḥ
tuṣṭuvū rāma-kṛṣṇau tau
vāgbhiḥ śruti-parāyaṇāḥ 5.8.46

“The sages and perfect beings, headed by Brahmā and all learned in the Vedas, came to see. With eloquent words they offered prayers to Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.”

tāḍayantya uro hastair
asti-prāpty-ādayaḥ striyaḥ
vinirgatas ta rurudur
jāta-vaidhavya-duḥkhitāḥ 5.8.47

“Kaṁsa’s wives, headed by Asti and Prāpti, beat their chests and wailed with grief.”

striya ūcuḥ ha nātha he yuddha-pate kva gato ’si mahā-bala
trailokya-vijayī sākṣād
devānām api durjayaḥ 5.8.48

Thw wives said: “O lord, O master of battle, O powerful one, where have you gone? You conquered the three worlds. Even the demigods could not defeat you.”

jāta-mātraḥ svasuḥ putrā
nirghṛṇena tvayā hatāḥ
anirdaśā nirdaśaś cā-
pare ’pi nihatā balāt 5.8.49

“You mercilessly killed your sisters sons when they were ten days old. Some were not even ten days old. Some you killed the moment they were born.”

tena pāpena ghoreṇa
daśām etādṛśīṁ gataḥ 5.8.50

“That horrible sin has brought you to this.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evam aśru-mukhīr dīnā
āśvāsya nṛpa-yoṣitaḥ
vidhāya yamunā-tīre
citāḥ śrīkhaṇḍa-samyutāḥ 5.8.51

Śrī Nārada said: “After consoling the poor tear-faced queens, the Lord built a sandalwood funeral pyre on the Yamunā’s shore.”

Chapter 9 - The Happiness of the Yadus

śrī-nārada uvāca
atha devau rāma-kṛṣṇau
devakī-vasudevayoḥ
samīpaṁ jagmatuḥ sākṣād
vṛṣīnbhiḥ parivāritau 5.9.1

Śrī Nārada said: “Then, accompanied by the Vṛṣṇis, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, the two Supreme Personalities of Godhead, approached Devakī and Vasudeva.”

svatas tayor bandhanāni
yayuḥ śithilitāṁ nṛpa
tau vīkṣya garuḍaṁ prāptaṁ
nāga-pāśa-guṇā yathā 5.9.2

“O king, seeing their condition, Lord Kṛṣṇa at once untied their bonds, as Garuḍa unties a knotted rope of snakes.”

sva-prabhāva-vidau vīkṣya
pitarau sa-balo hariḥ
sadyas tatāna svāṁ māyāṁ
jagan-moha-kariṁ balāt 5.9.3

“Seeing that Their parents had become aware of Their transcendental power, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma at once bewildered them with His illusory potency, which bewilders all the worlds.”

rāma-kṛṣṇau sutau jñātvā
śaurir moha-samākulaḥ
devakyā sahasotthāya
sasvaje cāśru-pūritaḥ 5.9.4

“Now thinking Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma his own sons, bewildered Vasudeva suddenly stood up. His eyes filled with tears, he and Devakī embraced their two sons.”

tāv āśvāsya hariḥ sadyo
vṛṣṇibhiḥ parivāritaḥ
mātāmahaṁ tūgrasenaṁ
cakāra mathurādhipam 5.9.5

“Accompanied by the Vṛṣṇis, Lord Kṛṣṇa consoled His parents. Then He crowned king of Mathurā His maternal grandfather, Ugrasena.”

āhūya yādavān kaṁsa-
bhayād deśāntaraṁ gatān
premṇā nivāsayām āsa
sa-kuṭumbān yadoḥ purī 5.9.6

“Calling together the Yādavas that had fled to other countries out of fear of Kaṁsa, Lord Kṛṣṇa affectionaltely gave homes in Mathurā to them and their families.”

nanda-rājaṁ gopa-gaṇaiḥ
sva-gṛhān gantum udyatam
natvā taṁ sa-balaḥ prāha
mohayann iva māyayā 5.9.7

“Then, bewildering them with Their illusory potency, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma bowed down before King Nanda and ther gopas, who were now eager to return to theor homes. Kṛṣṇa said to them:”

atraiva vāsaṁ kuru tāta puryāṁ
gantuṁ yadīcchā manasotthitā syāt
paścād ahaṁ vai sa-balo yadūn vā
vidhāya pārśvaṁ tava cāgamiṣye 5.9.8

“Father, please stay here in Mathurā for a while. If the desire to leave has risen in your heart, then please return to Vraja. Balarāma and I, with the Yādavas at Our side, will return there after a little while.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ śrī-rāma-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ
nanda-rājaḥ prapūjitaḥ
āliṅgya śauriṁ gopālair
yayau premāturo vrajam 5.9.9

Śrī Nārada said: “After being worshiped by Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, affectionate King Nanda embraced Vasudeva and then returned with the gopas to Vraja.”

dattaṁ śrī-kṛṣṇa-janmarkṣe
dhenūnāṁ niyutaṁ purā
brāhmaṇebhyo dadau śaurir
vastra-mālā-sv-alaṅkṛtam 5.9.10

“Then Vasudeva gave a million cows, along with many valuable garments and ornaments, in charity to the brāhmaṇas. This was the charity that previously he gave in meditation on the day of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s birth.”

śaurir gargaṁ samāhūya
śrī-kṛṣṇa-baladevayoḥ
yajñopavītaṁ vidhi-vat
kārayām āsa dharma-vit 5.9.11

“Then pious Vasudeva called Garga Muni and had him perform the sacred-thread ceremony for Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.”

rāma-kṛṣṇau sarva-vidyā-
dhyayanaṁ kartum udyatau
guroḥ sāndīpaneḥ pārśvaṁ
jagmatur janavat parau 5.9.12

“Then, as if They were ordinary boys eager to learn all things, the two Supreme Personalities of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, went to the home of Their guru, Sāndīpani Muni.”

kṛtvā paraṁ guroḥ sevaṁ
laghu-kālena mādhavau
sarva-vidyāṁ jagṛhatuḥ
sarva-vidyā-vidāṁ varau 5.9.13

“After rendering excellent service to Their guru, in a very short time Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, who were already the best of all-knowing philosophers, became learned in everything.”

gurave dakṣiṇāṁ dātuṁ
udyatau tau kṛtāñjalī
mṛtaṁ putraṁ dakṣiṇāyāṁ
tābhyāṁ vavre gurur dvijaḥ 5.9.14

“When Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma approached with folded hands to offer daksiṇā, Their brāhmaṇa guru chose the return of his dead son as his dakṣiṇā.”

ratham āruhya tau dantau
śātakumbha-paricchadam
prabhāse cābdhi-nikaṭaṁ
jagmatur bhīma-vikramau 5.9.15

“Riding in a golden chariot, powerful Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma came to Prabhāsa-kṣetra by the shore of the ocean.”

sadyaḥ prakampitaḥ sindhū
ratnopāyanam uttamam
nītvā tac-caraṇopānte
nipapāta kṛtāñjaliḥ 5.9.16

“At once the deity of the ocean appeared. Trembling, he offered a gift of jewels to Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma and with folded hands fell at Their feet.”

tam āha bhagavān śīghraṁ
putraṁ dehi guror mama
pracaṇḍormi-ghaṭāṭopais
tvayā tad-grahaṇaṁ kṛtam 5.9.17

“To him Lord Kṛṣṇa said, “Return at once My guru’s son, whom you took with your powerful waves.”

śrī-samudra uvāca
bhagavan deva-deveśa
na mayā bālako hṛtaḥ
hṛtaḥ pañcajanenāsau
śaṅkha-rūpāsureṇa vai 5.9.18

The ocean deity said: “O Lord, O master of the demigods, I did not take the boy. The conch-demon Pañcajana took him.”

vasan sadā mad-udare
baliṣṭho daitya-puṅgavaḥ
jetuṁ yogyas tvayā deva
devānāṁ bhaya-kārakaḥ 5.9.19

“O Lord, You are so powerful that You frighten even the demigods. Therefore it is proper that You defeat this very strong demon that always stays in my abdomen.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tenokto bhagavān kṛṣṇo
vāso baddhvā kaṭau dṛḍham
nipapāta mahā-vegāt
samudre bhīma-nādini 5.9.20

Śrī Nārada said: “When He heard these words, Lord Kṛṣṇa tightened the sash at His waist and dove into the ocean of terrible waves.”

śrī-kṛṣṇasya nipātena
tri-lokī-bhāra-dhāriṇaḥ
cakampe ’bdhir bhṛśaṁ vajrā-
kuṭeneva videha-rāṭ 5.9.21

“O king of Videha, when Lord Kṛṣṇa dove into it, the ocean, which holds up the three worlds, trembled as if it had been struck by a thunderbolt.”

tataḥ pañcajano daityo
yoddhuṁ śrī-kṛṣṇa-sammukhe
āgataḥ sahasā vīraḥ
śūlaṁ cikṣepa mādhave 5.9.22

“Eager to fight, the ferocious demon Pañcajana at once came before Lord Kṛṣṇa and threw a spear at Him.”

haste gṛhītvā tac-chūlaṁ
tenaivābhijaghāna tam
tad-ghātena prapatito
mūrchito vāri-maṇḍale 5.9.23

“Catching the spear in one hand, Lord Kṛṣṇa threw it back at the demon. Wounded by the spear, the demon fell unconscious into the waters.”

sahasotthāya deveśaṁ
kiñcid vyākula-mānasaḥ
mūrdhnā tatāḍa pakṣīndraṁ
sva-phaṇena phānī yathā 5.9.24

“Suddenly getting up, and now a little agitated at heart, with his head the demon struck Lord Kṛṣṇa as with its hood a snake strikes Garuḍa.”

paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān hariḥ
kruddho murdhani vegena
muṣṭinā taṁ tatāḍa ha 5.9.25

“Now angry, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, ferociously punched the demon on his head.”

kṛṣṇa-muṣṭi-prahāreṇa
sadyo vai nidhanaṁ gataḥ
taj-jyotiḥ śrī-ghanaśyāme
līnaṁ jātaṁ vidheha-rāṭ 5.9.26

“O king of Videha, with that punch the demon died. The light from his body then merged into the dark monsoon cloud of Lord Kṛṣṇa.”

evaṁ hatvā pañcajanaṁ
śaṅkhaṁ nītvā tad-aṅgajam
mahārṇavān nirgato ’sau
sahasā ratham āgamat 5.9.27

“After killing the demon, Lord Kṛṣṇa took the demon’s shell, left the great ocean, and returned to His chariot.”

vāyu-vegena yānena
rāma-kṛṣṇau manoharau
jagmatuḥ śamanasyāpi
dīrghaṁ samyamanīṁ purīm 5.9.28

“Traveling as fast as the wind for a long time, glorious Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma came to Samyamanī Purī, the city of Yamarāja.”

pāñcajanya-dhvanir lokaṁ
pracaṇḍo megha-ghoṣa-vat
pūrayām āsa taṁ śrutvā
cakampe sa-sabho yamaḥ 5.9.29

“Blowing the conchshell that had been Pa 241)cajana’s outer body, powerful Lord Kṛṣṇa filled the world with a sound like thunder. When they heard it, Yamarāja and his assembly trembled.”

catur-aśīti-lakṣeṣu
narakeṣu nipātitāḥ
yair yaiḥ śrutā dhvanis te te
jagmur mokṣaṁ tu pāpinaḥ 5.9.30

“Any of the sinners fallen in the eight million four hundred thousand hells there that heard that sound at once attained liberation.”

yamaḥ sadyo baliṁ nītvā
śrī-kṛṣṇa-baladevayoḥ
papāta caraṇopānte
dharṣitaḥ san kṛtāñjaliḥ 5.9.31

“Bringing an offering in his folded hands, humble Yamarāja fell down at the feet of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma.”

śrī-yama uvāca
he hare he kṛpā-sindho
rāma rāma mahā-bala
asaṅkhya-brahmāṇḍa-patī
paripūrṇatamau yuvām 5.9.32

Śrī Yamarāja said: “O Kṛṣṇa, O ocean of mercy! O Balarāma! O powerful Balarāma! You are the two masters of numberless universes. You are the two Supreme Personalities of Godhead.”

devau purāṇau puruṣau mahāntau
sarveśvarau sarva-jagaj-janeśau
adyaiva sarvopari vartamānau
girā nijājñāṁ vadataṁ pareśau 5.9.33

“You are the two ancient Supreme Persons, the masters of everything, the rulers of all beings in all the worlds, the two Supreme Lords who stand above all others. Please order me as You wish.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
guru-putraṁ loka-pāla
ānayasva mahā-mate
rājyaṁ kuru yathānyāyāṁ
mad-uktaṁ mānayan kvacit 5.9.34

The Blessed Lord said: “O protector of the worlds, O noble-hearted one, bring to Me my guru’s son. You should rule your kingdom by obeying My words.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tadaiva tenopānītaṁ
guru-putraṁ hariḥ svayam
gṛhītvāvantikam etya
dadau śrī-gurave śiśum 5.9.35

Śrī Nārada said: “Lord Kṛṣṇa then returned the boy to His guru.”

gurv-āśiṣā samyutau tau
natvā taṁ hi kṛtāñjalī
ratham āruhya mathurāṁ
āgatau yadu-pūjitau 5.9.36

“After receiving Their guru’s blessings, Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma bowed before him with folded hands. When, riding a chariot, They returned to Mathurā, the Yādavass greeted Them with honor and worship.”

ekadā sa-balaḥ kṛṣṇaḥ
sarva-kāraṇa-kārakaḥ
pāṇḍavān saṁsmaran bhaktān
akrūra-bhavanaṁ yayau 5.9.37

“Then, one day, remembering His devotees the Pāṇḍavas, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the cause of all causes, went with Balarāma to Akrūra’s home.”

akrūraḥ sahasotthāya
parirabhya mudānvitaḥ
upacāraiḥ ṣoḍaśabhiḥ
pūjayitvātha tau nṛpa 5.9.38

“Akrūra at once stood up and embraced Them. O king, he worshiped Them with sixteen offerings.”

kṛtāñjaliḥ puraḥ sthitvā
jāta-pūrṇa-manorathaḥ
uvācānanda-janitaṁ
muñcan bāṣpa-kalāṁ nṛpa 5.9.39

“O king, standing before Them with folded hands, shedding tears of joy, and all his desires now fulfilled, Akrūra spoke the following words.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
yuvābhyāṁ rāma-kṛṣṇābhyāṁ
tābhyāṁ nityaṁ namo namaḥ
yābhyāṁ mārge yad-uktaṁ me
pūrṇaṁ tac ca kṛtaṁ prabhū 5.9.40

Śrī Akrūra said: “O Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, again and again I offer my respectful obeisances to You. O my two Lords, what You told me on the path has now all become true.”

lokābhirāmau jana-bhūṣaṇottamau
cāntar bahiḥ sarva-jagat-pradīpakau
go-vipra-sādhu-śruti-dharma-devatā-
rakṣārtham adyaiva yadoḥ kule gatau 5.9.41

“You are the two joys of the worlds, the two ornaments of the people, the two lamps that light up all the universes and the realms beyond the universes. To protect the cows, brāhmaṇas, devotees, demigods, Vedas, and the principles of religion, You have both taken birth in the Yādava dynasty.”

kaṁsādi-daityendra-vināśa-hetave
goloka-lokāt paripūrṇa-tejasau
samāgatau bhārata-bhūmi-maṇḍale
yuvāṁ pareśau satataṁ nato ’smy aham 5.9.42

“To kill the demons headed by Kaṁsa, You have now come to this earth. You are the two Lords filled with power and glory. O two Supreme Personalities of Godhead, I bow down before You again and again.”

śrī-bhagavān uvāca
tvam arya-vṛddho dhṛtimān
ahaṁ tava puraḥ śiśuḥ
santo naḥ svātmanaḥ ślāghyaṁ
kurvanti hi mahā-mate 5.9.43

The Blessed Lord said: “You are a thoughtful and respected elder, and I am only a boy standing before you. O noble-hearted one, even the great saints cannot praise you enough.”

pāṇḍavānāṁ hi kuśalaṁ
draṣṭuṁ gaccha gajāhvayam
śīghram āgaccha tān dṛṣṭvā
sarvān dāna-pate bhavān 5.9.44

“Go at once to Hastināpura and see whether the Pāṇḍavas are happy and prosperous. O leader of the kind and generous, when you have seen everything, then quickly return here.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evam uktvā tadākrūraṁ
bhagavān bhakta-vatsalaḥ
sa-balaḥ śauri-bhavanaṁ
āyayau sarva-kārya-kṛt 5.9.45

Śrī Nārada said: “After speaking these words to Akrūra, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the supreme controller, who is kind to His devotees, went with Balarāma to Vasudeva’s palace.”

kauravendra-puraṁ gatvā-
krūro dṛṣṭvātha pāṇḍavān
punar āgatya kṛṣṇāya
vārtāṁ sarvām avarṇayat 5.9.46

“Akrūra went to Hastināpura, saw the Pāṇḍavas, returned, and related to Lord Kṛṣṇa all he had seen.”

śrī-akrūra uvāca
vinā yuvāṁ ko ’pi na pāṇḍavānāṁ
sahāya-kṛt kaurava-duḥkha-bhoginām
mṛte ca pāṇḍau bhavatoḥ padāmbuje
vilagna-cittā hi pṛthātmajā ye 5.9.47

Śrī Akrūra said: “Harassed by the Kauravas, the Pāṇḍavas have placed all their thoughts at Your lotus feet. Now that King Pāṇḍu is dead, no one but You can help them.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
iti śrutvākrūra-mukhāt
śrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān hariḥ
ardhaṁ rājyaṁ pāṇḍavebhyaḥ
kauravāṇāṁ balād dadau 5.9.48

Śrī Nārada said: “After hearing this from Akrūra’s mouth, Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, forced the Kauravas to give half their kingdom to the Pāṇḍavas.”

athoktaṁ vacanaṁ smṛtvā
tadoddhava-samanvitaḥ
mahā-maṅgala-samyuktaṁ
kubjāyā bhavanaṁ yayau 5.9.49

“Then, remembering His promise, the Lord went with Uddhava to Kubjā’s home.”

dṛṣṭvārāc chrī-hariṁ prāptaṁ
kubjā rūpavatī tvaram
bhaktyā samarhayām āsa
padyādyaiḥ prāṇa-vallabham 5.9.50

“Seeing that Lord Kṛṣṇa, who was more dear than life to her, had come, beautiful Kubjā worshiped Him with padya and other offerings.”

hema-ratna-khacit-kuḍye
kubjāyā bhavanottame
babhau hariḥ rūpavatyā
vaikuṇṭhe ramayā yathā 5.9.51

“As in Vaikuṇṭha He is glorious with the goddess of fortune, so in her palace of gold and jewels, Lord Kṛṣṇa was handsome and glorious, beautiful Kubjā by His side,”

paripūrṇatamaḥ sākṣāc
chrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān svayam
yasyāḥ patir abhūd rājann
aho tasyās tapo mahat 5.9.52

“O king, Kubjā must have performed many great austerities to obtain Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, as her lover.”

tatra sthitvā harir devo
dināny aṣṭau videha-rāṭ
āyayau śauri-bhavanaṁ
līlā-mānusa-vigrahaḥ 5.9.53

“O king of Videha, after staying there for eight days, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead who enjoys pastimes like those of a human being, returned to Vasudeva’s palace.”

Chapter 10 - The Stories of the Washerman, Weaver, and Florist

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
śrī-kṛṣṇa-caritaṁ puṇyaṁ
mayā tava mukhāc chrutam
punaḥ śrotuṁ manaś cādya
tṛṣito vā jalaṁ gataḥ 5.10.1

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “Although my heart has already heard Lord Kṛṣṇa’s sacred pastimes from your mouth, it thirsts to hear them again. Although it has already drunk this nectar, it thirsts to drink it again.”

kaṁsasya janma-karmāṇi
tvayoktāni śrutāni me
keśy-ādi-daitya-varyāṇāṁ
pūrva-janma-kṛtaṁ śrutam 5.10.2

“I have already heard from you the description of the deeds of Kaṁsa in his previous birth and the deeds of Keśī and other demons in their previous births.”

ko ’yaṁ tu rajakaḥ pūrvaṁ
avadhīd yaṁ hariḥ katham
aho yasya mahaj-jyotiḥ
kṛṣṇe līnaṁ babhūva ha 5.10.3

“Who was the washerman in his previous birth? Why did Lord Kṛṣṇa kill him? Why did his spiritual effulgence merge in Lord Kṛṣṇa’s body?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
tretā-yuge tv ayodhyāyāṁ
rāma-rājye videha-rāṭ
caraṇaṁ śṛṇvatāṁ kaścid
rajako hy avadat priyām 5.10.4

Śrī Nārada said: “O king of Videha, in Tretā-yuga, in Lord Rāma’s kingdom, in Ayodhyā, as Lord Rāma’s spies overheard, this same washerman said to his wife:”

nāhaṁ bibharmi tvāṁ duṣṭāṁ
uṣatīṁ para-veṣma-gām
strī-lobhī bibhryāt sītāṁ
rāmo nāhaṁ bhaje punaḥ 5.10.5

“I will not maintain you, a sinner that returns at dawn from another’s house. Rāmacandra, who is greedy to enjoy with His wife, may continue to maintain His Sītā, but I am not like Him.”

iti lokād bahu-mukhād
vākyaṁ śrutvātha rāghavaḥ
sītāṁ tatyāja sahasā
vane lokāpavādataḥ 5.10.6

“Hearing from many mouths that the people were criticizing Him, Lord Rāmacandra at once exiled Sītā to the forest.”

tasmai daṇḍaṁ dātum icchāṁ
na cakre rāghavottamaḥ
mathurāyāṁ dvāparānte
rajakaḥ sa babhūva ha 5.10.7

“Lord Rāmacandra had no wish to punish him, and in due course this person became a washerman in Mathurā at the end of Dvaspara-yuga.”

ku-vākya-dosa-śānty-arthaṁ
taṁ jaghāna hariḥ svayam
tad api pradadau mokṣaṁ
tasmai śrī-karuṇā-nidhiḥ 5.10.8

“To stop his blasphemy, however, Lord Kṛṣṇa killed him. Then the Lord, who is an ocean of mercy, gave him liberation.”

dayāloḥ kṛṣṇacandrasya
caritraṁ paramādbhutam
etat te kathitaṁ rājan
kiṁ bhūyaḥ śrotum icchasi 5.10.9

“O king, now I have spoke to you Lord Kṛṣṇacandra’s very wonderful pastimes. What more do you wish to hear?”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
purā vai vāyakaḥ ko ’pi
nitarāṁ muni-sattama
yasmai dadau ca sārūpyaṁ
śrī-kṛṣṇo bhagavān hariḥ 5.10.10

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “O best of sages, in his previous life who was the weaver that received from Lord Kṛṣṇa a transcendental form like His own?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
mithilā-nagare pūrvaṁ
vāyako hari-bhakti-kṛt
śrī-rāmodvāha-samaye
śiradhvaja-nṛpājñayā
rāma-lakṣmaṇa-veṣārthaṁ
vāsāṁsi racayan kila
laghu-sūtraiḥ parivayan
kuśalo vastra-karmasu 5.10.11-12

Śrī Nārada said: “In his previous life he was a weaver in Mithilā City. A great devotee of the Lord and an expert weaver, on King Śiradhvaja’s order he made very fine garments for Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa to wear on Lord Rāma’s wedding day.”

koṭi-kandarpa-lāvaṇyau
sundarau rāma-lakṣmaṇau
tau vīkṣya vāyako rājan
mohito ’bhūn mahā-manāḥ 5.10.13

“O king, when he saw Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa, who are more handsome than millions of Kāmadevas, the noble-hearted weaver became enchanted.”

ahaṁ sva-hastair vastrāṇi
tayor aṅgeṣu sarvataḥ
paridhānaṁ kārayāmi
cakre cetthaṁ manoratham 5.10.14

“In his heart he desired, “I wish with my own hands I could make garments to place on Their limbs.”

manasāpi varaṁ rāmo
dadau tasmā aśeṣa-vit
dvāparānte bhārate ca
bhaviṣyati manorathaḥ 5.10.15

“In His heart all-knowing Lord Rāma blessed him that his desire would be fulfilled in Bharata-varṣa at the end of Dvāpara-yuga.”

śrī-rāmasya varāt so ’yaṁ
mathurāyāṁ babhūva ha
tayor veṣaṁ kārayitvā
tat-sārūpyaṁ jagāma ha 5.10.16

“By the blessings of Lord Rāma he took birth in Mathurā City. He personally made garments for Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, and he attained a transcendental form like Theirs.”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
sudāmnā malinā brahman
kiṁ kṛtaṁ sukṛtaṁ vada
yad-gṛhaṁ jagmatuḥ sākṣād
rāma-kṛṣṇau manoharau 5.10.17

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “O brāhmaṇa, what pious deeds did the florist Sudāmā perform that handsome Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma personally came to his home? Please describe that to me.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
rāja-rāja-vanaṁ ramyaṁ
nāmnā caitrarathaṁ śubham
tasya vai puṣpa-baṭuko
hemamālīti-nāma-bhāk 5.10.18

“In Kuvera’s beautiful Caitraratha gardens there was a florist named Hemamālī.”

viṣṇu-bhakti-rataḥ śānto
dānī sat-saṅga-kṛn mahān
śrī-viṣṇudeva-prāpty-arthaṁ
deva-pūjāṁ cakāra ha 5.10.19

“He was peaceful, generous, and devoted to Lord Viṣṇu. He associated with saintly persons. He worshiped Lord Śiva to attain the company of Lord Viṣṇu.”

samāḥ pañca-sahasrāṇi
padmānāṁ ca śata-trayaṁ
nityaṁ nītvā dhūrjaṭaye
puro dhṛtvā nanāma ha 5.10.20

“For five thousand years he daily took three hundred lotus flowers, placed them before Lord Śiva, and bowed down.”

ekadāti-prasanno ’bhūt
try-abjakaḥ karuṇā-nidhiḥ
mālākāra mahā-buddhe
varaṁ brūhīty uvāca ha 5.10.21

“Lord Śiva, who is an ocean of mercy, was very pleased. One day he said, “Intelligent florist, ask for a boon.”

hemamālī tadā devaṁ
namaskṛtya kṛtāñjaliḥ
pradakṣiṇī-kṛtya puraḥ
sthitvā prāha natānanaḥ 5.10.22

“With folded hands bowing before Lord Śiva, circumambulating him, and standing before him with bowed head, Hemamālī spoke.”

śrī-hemamāly uvāca
paripūrṇatamaṁ kṛṣṇaṁ
kvacin no gṛham āgatam
paśyāmi dṛgbhyāṁ taṁ sākṣāt
tvad-vareṇa bhaved idam 5.10.23

Śrī Hemamālī said: “By your blessings may I be able to see with my own eyes the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa, enter my home.”

śrī-mahādeva uvāca
dvāparānte bhārate ca
mathurāyāṁ mahā-mate
manorathas te sa-phalo
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 5.10.24

Lord Śiva said: “O noble-hearted one, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga, in Bharata-varṣa, in Mathurā City, your desire will bear fruit. Of this there is no doubt.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
maheśvara-vareṇāsau
hemamālī mahā-manāḥ
mālākāro dvāparānte
sudāmā sambabhūva ha 5.10.25

Śrī Nārada said: “By Lord Śiva’s blessings, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga noble-hearted Hemamālī was born as the florist Sudāmā.”

Chapter 11 - The Stories of Kubjā and Kuvalayāpīḍa

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
sairandhryā kiṁ kṛtaṁ pūrvam
tapaḥ parama-durghaṭam
yena prasannaḥ śrī-kṛṣṇo
devair api su-durlabhaḥ 5.11.1

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “What severe austerities did the maidservant Kubjā perform in her previous birth that she so pleased Lord Kṛṣṇa, whom even the demigods cannot attain?”

pañcavatyāṁ sthitaṁ rāmaṁ
koṭi-kandarpa-sannibham
vīkṣya śūrpanakhā nāmnī
rakṣasī mohitā bhṛśam 5.11.2

“Gazing at Lord Rāma in Pañcavatī forest, a demonness name Śūrpanakhā fell passionately in love with Him.”

nirmohaṁ rāghavaṁ dṛṣṭvā-
thaika-patnī-vrata-sthitaṁ
krodhāt sītāṁ bhakṣayituṁ
dhāvatī rāvaṇa-svasā 5.11.3

“Seeing that Lord Rāma had vowed to accept only one wife, and had not fallen in love with her, Rāvaṇa’s sister Śūrpanakhā angrily ran to devour Sītā-devī.”

khaḍgena śita-dhāreṇa
lakṣmaṇo rāghavānujaḥ
jahāra tasyāḥ karṇau ca
nāsaṁ sadyo ruṣānvitaḥ 5.11.4

“At this Lord Rāma’s younger brother Lakṣmaṇa became angry and with a sharp-edged sword quickly cut away her ears and nose.”

chinna-nāsā gatā laṅkāṁ
ravanāya nyavedayat
bhūyaḥ puṣkara-tīrthe sā
jagāma vimanā bhṛśam 5.11.5

“Her nose cut away, she went to Laṅkā and told Rāvaṇa what had happened. Then, unhappy at heart, she went to Puṣkara-tīrtha.”

tapaś cakre śūrpanakhā
varṣāṇām ayutaṁ jale
dhyāyantī try-ambakaṁ devaṁ
śrī-rāmaṁ varam icchatī 5.11.6

“Wishing to get Lord Rāma as her husband, for ten thousand years Śūrpanakhā stayed underwater and meditated on Lord Śiva.”

tataḥ prasanno bhagavān
deva-deva uma-patiḥ
etya tat puṣkaraṁ tīrthaṁ
varaṁ brūhīty uvāca ha 5.11.7

“Pleased by this, Lord Śiva, who is the master of the demigods and the husband of Umā, came to Puṣkara-tīrtha and said, “Ask for a boon.”

śūrpanakhovāca śrī-rāmo me varo bhūyād
varaṁ dehi satāṁ priyaḥ
tvaṁ deva-deva paramaḥ
sarvāsām āśiṣāṁ prabhuḥ 5.11.8

Śūrpanakhā said: “You are the great master of the demigods. You are dear to the great saints. You have the power to give all benedictions. Please grant me that Śrī Rāma will become my husband.”

śrī-śiva uvāca
adyaiva sa-phalo na syād
varas te śṛṇu rākṣasi
dvāparānte māthure ca
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 5.11.9

Lord Śiva said: “O demonness, please listen. At the present time your boon cannot be granted. In the future, at the end of Dvāpara-yuga, in Mathurā City, it will be granted to you. Of this there is no doubt.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
saiva śūrpanakhā nāma rākṣasī kāma-rūpiṇī
abhūc chrī-mathurāyāṁ tu
kubjā-nāma mahā-mate 5.11.10

Śrī Nārada said: “O noble-hearted one, in Mathurā City the demonness Śūrpanakhā, who could assume any form at will, became the girl named Kubjā.”

mahādeva-vareṇāpi
śrī-kṛṣṇasya priyābhavat
idaṁ mayā te kathitaṁ
kiṁ bhūyaḥ śrotum icchasi 5.11.11

“By Lord Śiva’s blessing she became Lord Kṛṣṇa’s beloved. Now I have explained this to you. What more do you wish to hear?”

śrī-bahulāśva uvāca
ko ’yaṁ kuvalayāpīḍaḥ
pūrva-janmani nārada
kathaṁ gajatvam āpannaḥ
śrī-kṛṣṇe līnatāṁ gataḥ 5.11.12

Śrī Bahulāśva said: “O Nārada, who was Kuvalayāpīḍa in his previous birth? Why did he become an elephant? Why did he merge into Śrī Kṛṣṇa?”

śrī-nārada uvāca
bali-putro mahā-kāyo
nāmnā mandagatir balī
sarva-śastra-bhṛtāṁ śreṣṭho
lakṣa-nāga-samo balī 5.11.13

Śrī Nārada said: “Bali Mahārāja’s son was a giant named Mandagati. Strong as a hundred thousand elephants, he was the best of warriors bearing weapons.”

ekadā nirgataḥ so ’pi
raṅga-yātrāṁ janeṣu ca
mattebha-vaj janān vegād
bhujābhyāṁ parimardayan 5.11.14

“One day, when he was on a pilgrimage to Raṅga-kṣetra, he pushed his way through a crowd, pressing people with both arms as if he were a maddened wild elephant.”

tad-bahu-vegāt patitaḥ
pathi vṛddhas trito muniḥ
kruddhaḥ saśāpa taṁ mattaṁ
baliṣṭhaṁ bali-nandanam 5.11.15

“Pushed to the ground in this way, the elderly sage Trita Muni became angry and cursed Bali’s wild and powerful son.”

śrī-trita uvāca
gajavat tvaṁ madonmatto
’bhūr janān parimardayan
vicaran raṅga-yatrāyāṁ
tvaṁ gajo bhava durmate 5.11.16

Śrī Trita said: “Pushing the people on the way to Raṅga-kṣetra, you have become like a wild elephant. Fool, then I curse you to become an elephant.”

evaṁ śaptas tadā daityo
nāmnā mandagatir balī
patat kañcuka-vad deho
bhraṣṭa-teja babhūva ha 5.11.17

“Cursed in this way, the powerful Daitya named Mandagati at once shed his body as one sheds a garment. Now his power and splendor were all gone.”

muneḥ prabhāva-vit sadyo
daityo bhūtvā kṛtāñjaliḥ
natvā pradakṣiṇī-kṛtya
tritaṁ munim uvāca ha 5.11.18

“Now aware of the sage’s great power, Mandagati bowed with folded hands before him, circumambulated him, and then spoke.”

mandagatir uvāca
he mune he kṛpā-sindho
tvaṁ yogīndro dvijottamaḥ
gajatvān me kadā muktir
bhaviṣyati vadāśu mām 5.11.19

Mandagati said: “O sage, O ocean of mercy, You are the king of yogīs. You are the the best of brāhmaṇas. When will I become free from being an elephant. Please tell me.”

tvādṛśānāṁ satāṁ mābhūd
dhelanaṁ me kvacin mune
tvādṛśā munayo brahman
samarthā vara-śapayoḥ 5.11.20

“O sage, I should never have offended a great saint like yourself. O brāhmaṇa, sages like yourself have the power to bless or to curse.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
evaṁ prasāditas tena
trito nāma mahā-muniḥ
gata-krodho ’bravīd daityaṁ
kṛpālur brāhmaṇottamaḥ 5.11.21

Śrī Nārada said: “Pacified by this, and his anger now gone, the kind and exalted brāhmaṇa sage Trita Muni spoke to the Daitya.”

śrī-trita uvāca
vacanaṁ me mṛṣā na syāt
tvad-bhaktyā harṣito ’smy aham
te dāsyāmi varaṁ divyaṁ
devānām api durlabham 5.11.22

Śrī Trita said: “I am pleased by your devotion. Still, my words cannot become false. I will give you a transcendental blessing even the demigods cannot attain.”

mā śokaṁ kuru daityendra
mathurāyāṁ hareḥ puri
śrī-kṛṣṇa-hastāt te muktir
bhaviṣyati na saṁśayaḥ 5.11.23

“O king of the Daityas, don’t lament. In Lord Kṛṣṇa’s city of Mathurā you will attain liberation from Lord Kṛṣṇa’s own hand.”

śrī-nārada uvāca
so ’yaṁ mandagatir daityo
gajo ’bhūd vindhyā-parvate
nāmnā kuvalayāpīḍo
nāgāyuta-samo bale 5.11.24

Śrī Nārada said: “In this way the Daitya Mandagati became an elephant in the Vindhyā Hills. He was as strong as ten thousand other elephants.”

gṛhīto māgadhendreṇa
balāl lakṣa-gajair vane
so ’yaṁ dattas tu kaṁśāya
pāribarhe videha-rāṭ 5.11.25

“O king of Videha, with the aid of a hundred thousand elephants King Jarāsandha captured him and eventually gave him to Kaṁsa as a wedding present.”

Chapter 12 - Pancajana’s Previous Birth

sri-bahulacva uvaca canuradyac ca ye mallas te ke purvam ihagatah aho sri-krsnacandrena yesam yuddham babhuva ha sri-bahulacva 5.12.1

Sri Bahulacva said: “In their previous births who were Canura and the other wrestlers that fought with Lord Krsna?”

sri-narada uvaca rajan puramaravatyam utathyo ’sti maha-munih tasyabhavan panca putrah kamadeva-sama-prabhah sri-narada 5.12.2

Sri Narada said: “O king, in ancient times there was a great sage named Utathya, who lived in Amaravati. He had five sons handsome as Kamadeva.”

hitva vidyam cadhyayanam japam tena sahaiva te gatva baler malla-yuddham sadaciksan madoddhatah 5.12.3

“Abandoning their studies and mantra-chanting, these wild sons went to King Bali’s wrestling camp and studied wrestling daily.”

brahma-karma-paribhrastan vedadhyayana-varjitan rusa praha sa tan mattan utathyo muni-sattamah brahma-karma 5.12.4

“Utathya, the best of sages, angrily spoke to his sons, who had neglected their Vedic studies and brahminical duties.”

sri-utathya uvaca camo damas tapah caucam ksantir arjavam eva ca jnanam vijnanam astikyam brahma-karma sva-bhava-jam sri-utathya 5.12.5

Sri Utathya said: “Peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, wisdom, knowledge, and religiousness-these are the qualities by which the brahmanas work *.”

cauryam tejo dhrtir daksyam yuddhe capy apalayanam danam icvara-bhavac ca ksatram karma sva-bhava-jam 5.12.6

“Heroism, power, determination, resourcefulness, courage in battle, generosity, and leadership are the qualities of work for the ksatriyas *.”

krsi-go-raksya-vanijyam vaicya-karma sva-bhava-jam paricaryatmakam karma cudrasyapi sva-bhava-jam 5.12.7

“Farming, cow-protection, and business are the qualities of work for the vaicyas, and for the cudras there is labor and service to others *.”

brahma-karma-parityakta bhavanto brahmanah sutah malla-yuddham ksatra-yuddham katham kuruta durjanah brahma-karma 5.12.8

“Rogues, why do you, a brahmana’s sons, reject your brahminical duties and engage in wrestling, a ksatriya’s work.”

tasmad bhavanto bhuyasur malla vai bharatajire asuranam prasaggena durjana bhavatacu hi 5.12.9

“I curse you to become wrestlers in Bharata-varsa. By associating with demons you will become demons yourselves.”

sri-narada uvaca utathyasya sutas te vai jata malla mahi-tale sri-krsnagga-sparca-matrat param moksam yayur nrpa sri-narada 5.12.10

Sri Narada said: “In this way Utathya’s sons were born as wrestlers on the earth. O king, simply by the touch of Lord Krsna’s body they attained liberation.”

canuro mustikah kutah calas tocala eva ca esam caritram kathitam kim bhuyah crotum icchasi 5.12.11

“In this way I have described the activities of Canura, Mustika, Kuta, Sala, and Tocala. What more do you wish to hear?”

sri-bahulacva uvaca kamsanuja bhrataro ’stau kagka-nyagrodhakadayah te ke purvam vada mune ye ’pi moksam param gatah sri-bahulacva 5.12.12

Sri Bahulacva said: “Kamsa’s had eight younger brothers, headed by Kagka and Nyagrodha, who attained liberation. Who were they in their previous births. O sage, please tell me this.”

sri-narada uvaca alakayam pura yakso devayaksa iti smrtah jnani jnana-paro manyah civa-bhaktya maha-dyutih sri-narada 5.12.13

Sri Narada said: “In ancient times there was a Yaksa named Devayaksa, who lived in Alaka-puri. He was wise, learned, glorious, and sincerely devoted to Lord Siva.”

tasya castau suta jata devakuto mahagirih gando dandah pracandac ca khando ’khandah prthus tatha 5.12.14

“He had eight sons, who were named Devakuta, Mahagiri, Ganda, Danda, Pracanda, Khanda, Akhanda, and Prthu.”

ekada civa-pujayam devayaksena noditah sahasram pundarikani cahartum arunodaye 5.12.15

“One time Devayaksa sent them to pick, for the worship of Lord Siva, a thousand lotus flowers at sunrise.”

puspani manasan nitva cabditani madhu-vrataih aghraya gandha-lobhena dadus te janakaya vai 5.12.16

“From Manasa-sarovara, they took many flowers filled with the humming of bees. After greedily smelling them, they gave them to their father.”

ucchisti-krta-dosena civa-puja tiras-krta asurim yonim apanna mudhas te janmabhis tribhih ucchisti 5.12.17

“The worship of Lord Siva became ruined by offering these already-smelled flowers. The foolish sons became demons for three births.”

hastabhyam sagkarabhyam ca baladevasya maithila param moksam gatas te vai dosan mukta videha-rat 5.12.18

“O king of Videha, by the touch of Lord Balarama’s auspicious hands the sons became free of their sin and attained liberation.”

kamsanujanam vyakhyanam purva-janma-bhavam nrpa idam maya te kathitam kim bhuyah crotum icchasi 5.12.19

“O king, in this way I have described to you the previous births of Kamsa’s eight younger brothers. What more do you wish to hear?”

sri-bahulacva uvaca ko ’yam pura pancajano daityah cagkha-vapur-dharah tasya cagkho babhau brahman sri-krsna-kara-pagkaje sri-bahulacva 5.12.20

Sri Bahulacva said: “The demon Pancajana, who had the body of a conch, became the conchshell in Lord Krsna’s lotus hand. Who was he in his previous birth?”

sri-narada uvaca puraivaitany upaggani cakradini videha-rat trailokya-nathasya harer babhuvus tejasa hrtah sri-narada 5.12.21

Sri Narada said: “O king of Videha, Lord Krsna, the master of the three worlds, obtained His conch and other paraphernalia long ago by His own prowess.”

tesam cagkhah pancajanyah prapto rajan mahat-padam papau tan-mukha-lagno ’sau sri-krsnasyadharamrtam 5.12.22

“O king, among them the conchshell Pancajanya was especially exalted. Touching His mouth, again and again he would drink the nectar of Lord Krsna’s lips.”

akaroc caikada manam manasi praha cagkha-rat grhito ’ham hi harina rajahamsa-sama-dyutih 5.12.23

“Then one day he became proud and in his heart he said to himself: “I am the king of conchshells. I am effulgent as the king of swans. Lord Krsna personally holds me.”

sri-krsno daksinavartam dadhmau mam vijaye sati yad durlabham cabdhi-putryah sri-krsnasyadharamrtam sri 5.12.24

““When He is victorious, Sri Krsna points me to the south and makes a great sound through me. At that moment I drink the nectar of Lord Krsna’s lips, which is diificult even for the goddess of fortune to obtain.”

tat tasmat sarva-mukhyo ’smi pibamy aham ahar-nicam iti mana-yutam cagkham pancajanyam videha-rat cacapa laksmis tam krodhat tvam daityo bhava durmate so ’pi pancajano nama daityo ’bhut saritam patau 5.12.25-26

“Therefore I am the most important of all. Day and night I drink the nectar of Lord Krsna’s lips.” The goddess of fortune then angrily cursed him, Fool! Become a demon! You will become a demon named Pa 24cajana and you will live in the ocean.”

Chapter 13 - The Arrival of Sri Uddhava

sri-bahulacva uvaca agre cakara kim karyam mathurayam yaduttamah nivasayitva sva-jnatin vadaitan muni-sattama sri-bahulacva 5.13.1

Sri Bahulacva said: “After He gave homes in Mathura to His relatives, what did Lord Krsna do then? O best of sages, please tell me that.”

sri-narada uvaca paripurnatamah saksad bhagavan bhakta-vatsalah sasmara gokulam dinam gosa-gopala-sagkulam sri-narada 5.13.2

Sri Narada said: “Then the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, who loves His devotees, remembered unhappy Gokula, filled with cows and cowherd people.”

ekadahuya rahasi sakhayam bhaktam uddhavam uvaca bhagavan devah prema-gadgadaya gira 5.13.3

“One day, calling His devotee and friend Uddhava to a private room, the Supreme Personality of Godhead spoke with words choked with emotion.”

sri-bhagavan uvaca gaccha cighram vrajam he sakhe sundaram sri-lata-kunja-punjadibhir manditam caila-krsna-prabha-caru-vrndavanam gopa-gopi-ganair gokulam sagkulam sri-bhagavan 5.13.4

The Blessed Lord said: “Friend, please go at once to beautiful Vraja, which is decorated with many beautiful forest groves and flowering vines, which has beautiful Vrndavana forest glorious with Govardhana Hill and the Yamuna, and which is filled with gopas and gopis.”

eka-patram tu nandaya vai diyatam va dvitiyam yacoda-kare caiva bhoh va trtiyam tv idam radhikayai sakhe tatra gatva hi tan-mandiram sundaram eka 5.13.5

“There please give a letter to King Nanda. Place a second letter in Yacoda’s hand. Then, O friend, go to Radha’s beautiful palace and give a letter to Radha.”

va caturtham sakhibhyah cicubhyah cubham kaucalam diyatam patram evam prthak gopikanam catebhyac ca yuthebhya unmohitanam ca deyani patrani ca 5.13.6

“Give a fourth splendid and auspicious letter to My childhood gopa friends. Give letters to each of the hundreds of groups of gopis bewildered with love for Me.”

me pita nanda-rajo ghrni man-mana me ca mata yacoda smaraty acu mam vakya-vrndaih cubhair niti-vit tvam tayor me param pritim arad dvayor avaha 5.13.7

“My kind father, King Nanda, places all his thoughts on Me. My mother, Yacoda, always thinks of Me. You are expert in speaking many graceful words. Please carry My love to them.”

mat-priya radhika mad-viyogatura manyate mam vina kham jagan mohatah mad-viyogadhim asya mad-uktaih padair mocaya tvam bhavan daksino vak-pathe 5.13.8

“Radha, My beloved, is tortured by My absence. In My absence She thinks the entire world is a great expanse of empty sky. Speak My words to Her and free Her from Her suffering in My absence. You are expert in walking on the path of eloquent words.”

gopa-balah sudamadayo mat-priya mam sakhayam vina te ’pi mohaturah dehi tesam sukham mitra-vac chri-vraje sv-alpa-kalena tatragamisyamy aham gopa 5.13.9

“The gopa boys are also very dear to Me. Without Me, their friend, they are bewildered and unhappy. As a friend does, make them happy. Very soon I will return to beautiful Vraja.”

gopika mad-viyogadhi-vegatura man-manaskac ca mat-prapta-dehasavah ya mad-arthe ca santyakta-lokabalas tah katham natra mantrin bibharmi svatah 5.13.10

“The gopis are tortured by My absence. Their thoyghts are fixed on Me. Their bodies and lives are dedicated to Me. For My sake they have renounced the entire world. Counselor, how can I not protect and maintain them?”

ta asun tyaktum atrodyata uddhava yabhir adyapi krcchrair ghrtac casavah mad-viyogadhim asam mad-uktaih padair mocaya tvam bhavan daksino vak-pathe 5.13.11

“O Uddhava, they are on the verge of giving up their lives. It is very difficult for them to remain alive. Speak My words to them and free them from their suffering in My absence. You are expert in walking on the path of eloquent words.”

yena purvam vrajad agato ’ham sakhe tam ratham sacva-sutam ranad-ghantikam me ca sarupyam adyaiva pitambaram vaijayantim sahasra-cchadam pagkajam kundale divya-ratna-prabha-mandite koti-balarka-diptam manim kaustubham me maha-nadini caru-vamcim cubham puspa-yuktam ca yastim jagan-mohinim candanam sundaram divya-gandhavrtam barha-malladi-vesam kvanan-nupuram maulim evam grhanaggade uddhava gaccha gacchacu cadyaiva mad-vakyatah 5.13.12-14 sri-narada uvaca ity ukta uddhava cighram namaskrtya krtanjalih krsnam pradaksini-krtya ratharudho vrajam yayau sri-narada 5.13.15

“Hearing these words, with folded hands Uddhava bowed before Lord Krsna, circumambulated Him, and at once rode on a chariot to Vraja.”

koticah kotico gavah yatra yatra manoharah cveta-parvata-sagkaca divya-bhusana-bhusitah 5.13.16

“Wherever he went in Vraja there were millions and millions of beautiful cows glorious as great white mountains and decorated with splendid ornaments.”

payasvinyas tarunyac ca cila-rupa-gunair yutah sa-vatsah pita-pucchac ca vrajantyo bhavya-murtikah 5.13.17

“They were young, virtuous, full of milk, and accompanied by their calves. They were beautiful and their tails were yellow.”

ghanta-manjita-jhagkarah kigkini-jala-manditah hema-tulya hema-crggyo hara-malah sphurat-prabhah ghanta-manjita 5.13.18

“They were deocrated with networks of tinkling bells and ornaments and with garlands and necklaces. Their bodies and horns were like gold.”

patalah haritas tamra pitah cyama vicitritah dhumrah kokila-varnac ca yatra gavas tv anekadha 5.13.19

“Some cows were pink, some green, some brown, some multi-colored, some yellow, some smoke-colored, and some the color of cuckoos.”

samudra-vad dugdha-dac ca taruni-kara-citritah kuragga-vad vilagghadbhir go-vatsair manditah cubhah samudra 5.13.20

“They were decorated with pictures of girls’ handprints. They gave milk that was like an ocean. They were decorated with calves leaping like deer.”

itas tatac calantac ca go-ganesu maha-vrsah dirgha-kandhara-crggadhya yatra dharma-dhurandharah itas 5.13.21

“Wandering here and there among the cows were great bulls with broad shoulders and long horns, bulls bearing the principles of religion.”

gopala vetra-hastac ca cyama-vamci-dharah parah krsna-lilah pragayanto ragair madana-mohanaih 5.13.22

“There were many gopas playing black flutes and, with melodies that could charm Kamadeva himself, singing of Lord Krsna’s pastimes”

durat tam agatam viksya jnatva krsnam vrajarbhakah ucuh parasparam te vai krsna-darcana-lalasah 5.13.23

“Seeing Uddhava coming, and thinking he was Krsna Himself, the gopa boys, eager to see Krsna, spoke among among themselves.”

sri-gopa ucuh nanda-sunuh kilayati sakha yo ’yam na samcayah megha-cyamah pita-vasah sragvi kundala-manditah sri-gopa 5.13.24

The gopas said: “Nanda’s son is coming. It is our friend Krsna. There is no doubt of it. He is dark as a monsoon cloud. He wears yellow garments. He is decorated with earrings and a garland.”

kaustubhi kundali bibhrat sahasra-dala-pagkajam tad eva mukutam bibhrat koti-martanda-sannibham 5.13.25

“He wears earrings and a Kastubha jewel. He holds a thousand-petal lotus. He wears a crown splendid as millions of suns.”

ta evacva rathah so ’yam kigkini-jala-manditah balo nasti rathe casminn ekaki nanda-nandanah 5.13.26

“These are the same horses. It is the same chariot decorated with a network of tinkling ornaments. Balarama is not on it. Nanda’s son has come alone.”

sri-narada uvaca evam vadanto gopalah cridamadya videha-rat krsnakrtim krsna-sakham ayayuh sarvato ratham sri-narada 5.13.27

Sri Narada said: “O king of Videha, speaking in this way, the gopas headed by Sridama surrounded the chariot Krsna’s friend, whose form was like Krsna’s.”

krsno nastiti vadatah ko ’yam saksat tad-akrtih tan namaskrtyaupagavih parirabhyavadat pathi 5.13.28

“The gopas said, “He is not Krsna. Who is he? His form is like Krsna’s.” Then, in the middle of the road, Uddhava bowed before the gopas and embraced them all.”

sri-uddhava uvaca grhana patram cridaman krsna-dattam na samcayah cokam ma kuru gopalaih kucaly aste harih svayam sri-uddhava 5.13.29

Sri Uddhava said: “O Sridama, please take this letter. Krsna gave it. Of this there is no doubt. Don’t lament. Krsna takes a personal interest in the welfare of the gopas.”

yadavanam mahat karyam krtvatha sa-balah prabhuh hrasva-kalena catrapi bhagavan agamisyati 5.13.30

“After He has fulfilled His responsibilities to the Yadavas, Lord Krsna and Balarama will quickly erturn to Vraja.”

sri-narada uvaca pathitva tad-dhasta-patram cridamadya vrajarbhakah bhrcam acruni muncantah prahur gadgadaya gira sri-narada 5.13.31

Sri Narada said: “After reading the letter in their hands, the boys of Vraja burst into tears. Then, their voices choked with emotion, they spoke.”

sri-gopa ucuh pantheti nirmohini nanda-sunau tanur vibhutic ca dhanam balam ca sarva dhiyah krsnam rte vraje nah cunyam prajatam hi jagat samastam sri-gopah 5.13.32

The gopas said: “O traveler, Krsna’s body, opulence, wealth, strength, intelligence are never thwarted or bewildered. For us people of Vraja, to be without Krsna is to live in a world that is a desert.”

ksano yugatvam va ghati maha-mate prayati manvantaratam vrajaukasam yamac ca kalpam ca dinam vina harim viyoga-duhkhair dvi-parardhatam gatam 5.13.33

“O noble-hearted one, for the people of Vraja suffering in Krsna’s absence, a moment has become a yuga, a half-hour has become a manvantara, three hours has become a kalpa, and a day has become a lifetime of Brahma.”

Chapter 14 - The Meeting of King Nanda and Uddhava

sri-narada uvaca evam prema-bharan gopan chri-krsna-virahaturan uvaca prema-samyukta uddhavo gata-vismayah sri-narada 5.14.1

Sri Narada said: “Uddhava, who dearly loved Lord Krsna, was not surprised to hear all this. Then he spoke to the gopas, who were overcome with love for Krsna and were tornmented by His absence.”

sri-uddhava uvaca aham sri-krsna-daso ’smi tat-priyas tad-rahaskarah bhavatam kucalam drastum presito harina tvaram sri-uddhava 5.14.2

Sri Uddhava said: “I am Sri Krsna’s servant. i am His close friend. He sent me here to see if you were happy.”

purim gatvatha haraye nivedya viraham tu vah tam prasannam karisyami tad-agghrau netra-varibhih 5.14.3

“I will return to Mathura City, tell Lord Krsna how you suffer in His absence, and soften His heart by placing my tears at His feet.”

natva harim hi bhavatam samipam he vrajaukasah agamisyamy aham cighram capatho na mrsa mama 5.14.4

“O people of Vraja, I will bow down before Lord Krsna and then I will quickly bring Him to you. My promise will not become false.”

yuyam prasanna bhavata ma cokam kurutatha vai asmin vraje ’pi gopala draksyatha sri-patim harim 5.14.5

“Be happy. Don’t lament. O gopas, in this land of Vraja you will again see Sri Krsna, the husband of the goddess of fortune.”

sri-narada uvaca evam acvasya gopalan ratha-stho yadu-nandanah cridamadyaic ca gopalaih sahito harsa-puritah viveca nanda-nagaram surye sindhu-gate sati sri-narada 5.14.6

Sri Narada said: “In this way Uddhava comforted the gopas. Then, as the sun was setting in the west, cheerful Uddhava, accompanied by Sridama and the gopas, entered Nanda’s village.”

agatam hy uddhavam crutva nanda-rajo maha-matih parirabhya muda cighram pujayam asa harsitah 5.14.7

“Hearing that he had come, King Nanda happily embraced and worshiped Uddhava.”

kacipu-stham sthitam cantam uddhavam krta-bhojanam kacipu-stho nanda-rajah praha gadgadaya gira kacipu 5.14.8

“When Uddhava was comfortably seated on a soft cushion and fed a nice meal, King Nanda, also seated on a cushion, spoke many words choked with emotion.”

sri-nanda uvaca kaccit sakha me puri curasena aste sva-putaih kucali maha-mate kamse mrte yadava-puggavanam jatam sakhe saukhyam atah param bhuvi sri-nanda 5.14.9

Sri Nanda said: “O noble-hearted one, are my friend Surasena and his sons happy in Mathura City? Now that Kamsa is dead the Yadavas must be very happy.”

kaccit kadacit sa-balo hi madhavah smaraty asau va jananim yacomatim gopala-govardhana-go-ganan vrajam vrndavanam va pulinam taragginim 5.14.10

“Do Krsna and Balarama sometimes remember Their mother, Yacoda? Do They remember the gopas, the cows, Vraja, Govardhana Hill, Vrndavana forest, and the shore of the Yamuna?”

ha daiva kasmin samaye sva-nandanam bimbadharam sundaram ambujeksanam draksyamy aham mandira-catvarajire ’rbhakair luthantam sa-balam muhur muhuh 5.14.11

“O destiny, when will I see my handsome, lotus-eyed, bimba-lip son playing again and again in the courtyard with Balarama and the boys?”

kunjo nikunjo yamuna maha-nadi govardhano ’ranyam idam vanani grhair lata-vrksa-gavam ganaih saha vina mukundam visavat tv idam jagat kunjo 5.14.12

“Without Krsna the groves and gardens, the great Yamuna river, Govardhana Hill, this forest, the other forests, our homes, the cows, trees, and flowering vines, and this entire universe are flooded with poison.”

dhig jivanam me cayanam ca bhojanam krsnam vina padma-dalayateksanam candram vina bhumi-tale cakoravaj jivami tasyagamanacaya bhrcam 5.14.13

“This life of eating and sleeping without Krsna, whose eyes are like great lotus petals. is wretched and miserable. I am like a cakora bird that cannot see the moon. I stay alive only by hoping that Krsna will return.”

hartum bhuvo bharam ativa daivataih samprarthitam purnatamam maha-mate jatam satam raksana-tat-param svayam manye hi krsnam sa-balam parat param 5.14.14

“I think that Lord Krsna is the original Supreme Personality of Godhead, greater than the greatest, and that, requested by the demigod Brahma, He has come here with Balarama to protect the devotees and remove the earth’s burden.”

sri-narada uvaca samsmrtya samsmrtya harim parecam babhuva tusnim nava-nanda-rajah ciro nidhayapy upabarhane sve hy utkantha-romancita-vihvalaggah sri-narada 5.14.15

Sri Narada said: “Thinking again and again of Lord Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, King Nanda became silent. The hairs of his body erect with his longing to see Krsna, he rested his head on a pillow.”

sri-nanda-netrambuja-vari-santati rajams tada krsna-sakhasya pacyatah cayyam sa-vastram upabarhanantam krtvardratam praggana acacala sri 5.14.16

“As Krsna’s friend Uddhava looked on, King Nanda drenched the pillow, his garments, and the chair with tears from his eyes. Then Nanda went into the courtyard.”

crutvoddhavam sri-mathura-puragatam kapatam etyacu yacomati sati crnvanty alam svasya sutasya varnanam sneha-sravat-su-stana-netra-pagkaja 5.14.17

“Hearing that Uddhava had come from Mathura City, saintly Yacoda rushed to the door. Milk flowing from her breasts and tears flowing from her lotus eyes, she overheard the description of her son.”

vihaya lajjam ghrnaya sutasya sa papraccha sarvam kucalam tadoddhavam aproksya vastrena drg-acru-santatim sthite ca nande hari-bhava-vihvale 5.14.18

“Rejecting all shyness, and overcome with love for her son, she asked Uddhava everything about Krsna’s well-being. Meanwhile Nanda was overcome with love. He wept, drenching his garments with tears from his eyes.”

sri-yacodovaca kvacit smarati mam krsno nanda-rajam athapi va bhrataram nanda-rajasya sannandam darcanotsukam sri-yacoda 5.14.19

Sri Yacoda said: “Does Krsna remember me? Does He remember King Nanda? Does He remember Nanda’s brother, Sannanda, who yearns to see Him?”

nandan navopanandamc ca vrsabhanun vrajesu sat yesam aroham asthaya bala-kelir vane vane 5.14.20

“Does He remember the Nandas, the nine Upanandas, and the six Vrsabhanus of Vraja? Doe He remember the boys He played with in forest after forest?”

kanduka-kridaya reme sanandam nanda-nandanah tan gopan sneha-samyuktan kadacit smarati svatah kanduka 5.14.21

“Does He sometimes remember the affectionate gopa boys with whom He enjoyed playing ball-games?”

eko ’yam me sutah prapto na suta bahavac ca me so ’pi mam jananim dinam yayau tyaktva dig-antaram 5.14.22

“I have only one son. I do not have many sons. Abandoning His poor mother, my son has gone away.”

aho kastam snehavatam durnivaram maha-mate kim karomi vina putram katham jivami manada 5.14.23

“O noble-hearted one, for they who love Him, this is a great calamity. O gentle one, what will I do without my son? How can I live?”

matar mahyam dehi dadhi matar haiyaggavam navam evam vadan sa madhuram hatham cakre sada grhe 5.14.24

““Mother, give Me yogurt. Mother, give Me fresh butter.” He would always speak these sweet words, and He would always do mischief in the house.”

madhyahne sa katham krsno bhojanam kartum arhati mamatmajo ’yam sri-krsno jivanam vraja-vasinam vraje dhanam kule dipo mohano bala-lilaya 5.14.25

“How He could eat at lunchtime! My son, Sri Krsna, is the wealth of Vraja, the life of Vraja’s people, and a brilliant lamp shining in His family. He is so sweet and so playful.”

lalanaih palanais tasya dinam me ksanavad gatam tad dinam kalpavaj jatam vinaho nanda-nandanam 5.14.26

“- Nanda’s son. Protecting and caressing Him, I thought each day was a single moment. Without Krsna my day is like a kalpa.”

vatsan varayitum krsno grama-simni nadi-tate na karito ’rbhakaih sardham sa caho mathuram gatah 5.14.27

“No longer do Krsna and the boys keep the calves on this side of the river, still close to the village. Now Krsna has gone to Mathura.”

he mohaneti durat tam agkam nitvatha lalanam cakara nanda-rajo ’yam tam vina khinnatam gatah 5.14.28

“From far away King Nanda would call “Sweet boy!”, place Him on his lap, and caress Him. Without Him, Nanda is filled with sadness.”

aho damna maya baddho nirmohinyaikada cicuh bhande bhagni-krte dadhnah cocami caritam ca tat 5.14.29

“One day the boy broke a yogurt pot and I carefully tied Him with a rope. Oh, how I regret that.”

tat-pragganam sarva-sabha ca mandiram dvarac ca vithir vraja-harmya-prsthayah cunyam samas tam mama jivanam dhig vina mukundam visavat tv idam jagat tat 5.14.30

“Without Krsna the courtyard, the assembly-house, my palace, the gateway, the pathway, and all the other palaces of Vraja have become an empty desert. Krsna is my whole life. Without Him this world is like a flood of poison.”

sri-narada uvaca yacoda-nandayor viksya paramam prema-laksanam uddhavo nitaram rajan vismito ’bhud gata-smayah sri-narada 5.14.31

Sri Narada said: “O king, when he saw Nanda’s and Yacoda’s great love, Uddhava became filled with wonder.”

sri-uddhava uvaca roma-matram mama tanau jihva cej jayate tv aho yuvayos tad api clagham kartum nalam maha-prabhu sri-uddhava 5.14.32

Sri Uddhava said: “You two are my masters. Even if the hairs on my body stood erect in ecstasy, my tongue would still not have the power to praise you properly.”

paripurnatame saksac chri-krsne purusottame idrci ca krta bhaktir yuvabhyam prema-laksana tirthatana-tapo-dana-sagkhya-yogaic ca durlabha cacvati yuvayoh prapta ya bhaktih prema-laksana 5.14.33-34

“The eternal love and devotion you both feel for Lord Krsna, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, cannot be attained by pilgrimages, austerity, charity, sagkhya, or yoga.”

ma cokam kuru re nanda he yacode vrajecvari patra-dvayam grhanacu krsna-dattam na samcayah 5.14.35

“O Nanda, don’t lament. O Yacoda, O queen of Vraja, take these two letters. They are letters from Krsna. Of this there is no doubt.”

sahagrajo nanda-sunuh kucaly aste yadoh puri yadavanam mahat karyam krtvatha sa-balah prabhuh hrasva-kalena catrapi bhagavan agamisyati 5.14.36

“Krsna and Balarama are happy in the city of the Yadavas. When Their mission is successfully completed, Krsna and Balarama will return here at once.”

paripurnatamam viddhi sri-krsnam nanda-nandanam kamsadinam vadharthaya bhaktanam raksanaya ca brahmana prarthitah krsno ’vatatara grhe tava jata-matro ’dbhutam lilam cakara sa-balo harih 5.14.37-38

“Know that Nanda’s son, Krsna, is the original Supreme Personality of Godhead. Requested by the demigod Brahma, and in order to protect the devotees and kill the demons led by Kamsa, He descended to your home. Since the moment of His birth, He and Balarama enjoyed many wonderful pastimes.”

putana-prana-haranam cakatasya nipatanam trnavarta-nipatac ca yamalarjuna-bhanjanam 5.14.39

“He drew out Putana’s life, overturned the cart, made Trnavarta fall, and broke the yamalarjuna trees.”

sva-mukhe ca yacodayai vicva-rupasya darcanam vrndavane ca bhagavan go-vatsamc carayan prabhuh sva 5.14.40

“The Lord showed to Yacoda the universal form in His mouth. The Lord herded the calves in Vrndavana forest.”

vadham cakara gopanam pacyatam baka-vatsayoh aghasurasya ca vadho dhenukasya vimardanam 5.14.41

“As the gopas watched, He killed Bakasura, Vatsasura, Aghasura, and Dhenukasura.”

mardanam kaliyasyapi vahni-panam cakara ha pralambasya vadham paccad baladevac cakara ha 5.14.42

“He defeated Kaliya and then he drank up the forest fire. Then Balarama killed Pralambasura.”

govardhanam samutpatya hastenaikena lilaya yusmakam pacyatam bibhrat puskaram gaja-rad iva 5.14.43 cuda-manim sagkhacudaj jahara jagatam patih aristasya vadham krtva kecinam nijaghana ha cuda 5.14.44

“Lord Krsna, the master of the universes, frocibly took Sagkhacuda’s crown jewel. Then He killed Keci and Aristasura.”

vyomasuram maha-daityam mustina tam mamarda ha tatha vai mathurayam tu cakre citram maha-mate 5.14.45

“O noble-hearted one. With His fist He killed the great demon Vyomasura. O noble-hearted one, in Mathura City He did many wonderful deeds.”

vikathyamanam rajakam karenabhijaghana tam pracandam kamsa-kodandam madhyatas tad babhanja ha iksu-dandam yatha nagah sarvesam pacyatam nrnam 5.14.46

“With one hand He killed an insulting washerman. As everyone watched, He broke Kamsa’s great bow in the middle as an elephant breaks a stalk of sugarcane.”

dvipam kuvalayapidam nagayuta-samam bale cunda-dande saggrhitva patayam asa bhu-tale 5.14.47

“Grabbing the trunk of Kuvalayapida elephant, who was a strong as ten thousand other elephants, Krsna threw it to the ground.”

canuram mustikam kutam calam tocalam eva ca patayam asa bhu-prsthe malla-yudhena madhavah 5.14.48

“As He wrestled with them, Lord Krsna threw Canura, Mustika, Kuta, Sala, and Tocala to the ground.”

kamsam madotkatam daityam naga-laksa-samam bale mancad grhitva tam krsno bhramayitva bhujaujasa patayam asa bhu-prsthe kamandalum ivarbhakah ibhopari yatha simhas tasyopari papata sah 5.14.49-50

“From the viewer’s box Lord Krsna dragged Kamsa, who was as strong as a hundred thousand elephants, whirled around with the power of His arms, and thew him to the ground as a child throws a kamandalu. Then Krsna jumped on him as a lion jumps on an elephant.”

kamsanujamc ca kagkadin baladevo maha-balah mamarda mudgarenacu mrgan vai mrga-rad iva 5.14.51

“Then with a club Lord Balarama killed Kamsa’s younger brothers, headed by Kagka, as a lion kills a herd of deer.”

gurave daksinam datum samutpatya maharnave cagkha-rupam pancajanam nijaghana harih svayam 5.14.52

“Then, so He could offer daksina to His guru, Lord Krsna dove into the ocean and killed Pancajana, who had the form of a conch.”

Chapter 15 - Seeing Sri Radha

sri-narada uvaca evam hi nandoddhavayor hareh kathayatoh katham vyatita ksanavad rajan ksanada harsa-vardhini sri-narada 5.15.1

Sri Narada said: “O king, as they talked about Lord Krsna, Nanda and Uddhava enjoyed a great festival of happiness, and the time seemed to pass like a single moment.”

brahme muhurte cotthaya gopyah sarva grhe grhe dehaly-agganam alipya dipams tatra nirupya ca brahme 5.15.2

“In each home the gopis rose at brahma-muhurta, anointed the doorways and courtyards, arranged the lamps, . . .”

praksalya hasta-padau ca methyam netram nidhaya ca mamanthuh sarvato yuktah picchilani dadhini tah 5.15.3

“. . . washed their hands and feet, looked for their churning-sticks, and began churning cream and yogurt.”

netrakarsa-calad-bhara-bhuja-kagkana-kigkinih venibhyo vigata-puspah sphurat-kundala-manditah 5.15.4

“As with their arms they pulled the churning-rope, their bracelets and other ornaments tinkled, their earrings glistened, and flowers fell from their braids.”

candra-mukhyah kanja-netras citra-varnair manoharah maggalani caritrani sri-krsna-baladevayoh gayantyah prema-samyukta yatra tatra grhe grhe ghose ghose cubha gavo rambhamana itas tatah 5.15.5-6

“As in every home they churned, the lotus-eyed, moon-faced, colorfully-dressed, beautiful, affectionate gopis sang with love about the auspicious pastimes of Krsna and Balarama. Then in every barn the beautiful cows began to moo.”

sarvatra gopika-gitam dadhi-cabdena micritam vithyam vithyam tatah crnvan vismitac coddhavo ’bravit 5.15.7

Hearing on every path the gopis’ songs and the sounds of yogurt being churned, Uddhava, filled with wonder, said:”

aho vai nanda-nagare bhaktir nrtyati yatra ca evam vadan bahir gramad yayau snatum nadi-jale 5.15.8

““Love for Krsna dances in Nanda’s village.” After speaking these words, Uddhava left the village to bathe in the river.”

sri-gopya ucuh kasyayam adyatra rathah samagato ’kruro ’thava krura utagatah punah yenaiva nito mathuram maha-purim sri-nanda-sunur nava-kanja-locanah sri-gopya 5.15.9

The gopis said: “Whose chariot is this? Has cruel Akrura, who took lotus-eyed Krsna to Mathura City returned?”

kasmin ku-kale janani sasarja yam datum satam snehavatam pratapanam kadrur yatha naga-cayam visavrtam hantum vrtha loka-janan itas tatah 5.15.10

“At what inauspicious moment did his mother, in order to give pain to the loving devotees, give birth to Akrura? She did it as Kadru, in order randomly to kill people here and there, gave birth to the species of snakes.”

kamsartha-krt kamsa-sakho ’ti-nirghrno so ’yam punah kim vraja-mandalam gatah bhartur mrtasyapi hi para-laukikim asmabhir adyaiva karisyati kriyam 5.15.11

“Has Kamsa’ cruel friend and servant, Akrura, come again to the circle of Vraja? Will he now perform his dead master Kamsa’s funeral rites for us?”

sri-narada uvaca evam vadantyo vraja-gopa-vadhvah santadya sutam ca mukhe ’ggulibhyam papracchur arad gata-buddhim artam tvaram vadaitat kila kasya yanam sri-narada 5.15.12

Sri Narada said: “Speaking these words, and slapping his face, the gopis asked the pained and bewildered charioteer, Whose chariot is this? Tell us at once!””

ghana-prabham padma-dalayateksanam krsnakrtim koti-manoja-mohanam pitambaram satpada-saggha-sagkulam malam dadhanam nava-vaijayantim sphurat-sahasra-cchada-padma-panim vamci-dharam vetra-karam manoharam balarka-koti-dyuti-mauli-mandanam maha-manim kundala-manditanam gaty-akrti-sri-tanu-hasa-su-svaraih sri-krsna-sarupya-dharam tam uddhavam vilokya sarva nrpa vismitas tato vijnaya govinda-sakham yayuh purah ghana 5.15.13-15

“At that moment seeing Uddhava, splendid as a monsoon cloud, His eyes lotus petals, His form like Sri Krsna’s, more handsome than millions of Kamadevas, dressed in yellow garments, wearing a new vaijayanti garland filled with bumblebees, holding in his hands a stick, flute, and thousand-petal lotus, wearing a crown and ornaments glorious as the rising sun, decorated with earrings and a great jewel, and gently laughing, the gopis became filled with wonder. O king, aware that this must be Sri Krsna’s friend, they approached him.”

jnatvatha sandeca-haram hareh prabhoh su-vakya-nitya paramadarena tam guptam hi prastum kucalam satam pateh nitvoddhavam tah kadali-vanam gatah 5.15.16

“Aware that he was carrying a letter from Lord Krsna, the gopis brought Uddhava to a secluded banana tree forest to ask him with sweet and respectful words about the welfare of Lord Krsna, the master of the devotees.”

yatraiva radha vrsabhanu-nandini krsna-tate caru-nikunja-mandire samasthita tad-virahatura bhrcam kham manyate sa tu jagad dharim vina 5.15.17

“King Vrsabhanu’s daughter Radha, who thought the entire universe an empty void in Lord Krsna’s absence, was there, in a beautiful forest-cottage by the Yamuna’s shore.”

rambha-dalaic candana-pagka-sancayam spharasphurac-chitala-megha-mandiram krsna-calac-caru-taragga-sikaram svatah sudha-racmi-galat-sudha-cayam etadrcam yat kadali-vanam ca tad radha-viyoganala-varcasa bhrcam babhuva sarvam satatam hi bhasmasat krsnagam acatma-tanum hi raksati rambha 5.15.18-19

“There, in the banana-tree forest, in a cooling monsoon-cloud banana-leaf cottage anointed with sandal paste and cooled by nectar moonlight and mist from the Yamuna’s graceful and restless waves, Sri Radha was burned by flames of separation from Sri Krsna. Only the hope that Sri Krsna would return kept Her alive.”

crutvoddhavam krsna-sakham samagatam cakara radha sva-sakhibhir adaram jalacanadyair madhuparka-maggalaih sri-krsna krsneti muhur vadanty alam 5.15.20

“Hearing that Krsna’s friend Uddhava had come, Radha and Her friends greeted him with great respect. She offered him a seat, water, madhuparka, and many other things. Again and again She said, “Sri Krsna! O Sri Krsna!”

radham hi govinda-viyoga-khinnam kuhvam yatha candra-kalam tadoddhavah natam krcaggim krta-hasta-samputah pradaksini-krtya jagada harsitah 5.15.21

“Radha, tormented in separation from Lord Krsna, and now slender as the new moon, bowed before Uddhava. With folded hands Uddhava circumambulated Her and cheerfully spoke.”

sri-uddhava uvaca sadasti krsnah paripurna-devo radhe sada tvam paripurna-devi sri-krsnacandrah krta-nitya-lilo lilavati tvam krta-nitya-lila sri-uddhava 5.15.22

Sri Uddhava said: “Eternally Sri Krsna is the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead. Eternally You are His supreme goddess. Eternally Sri Krsna enjoys transcendental pastimes. Eternally You enjoy transcendental pastimes with Him.”

krsno ’sti bhuma tvam asindira sada brahmasti krsnas tvam asi svara sada krsnah civas tvam ca civa civartha visnuh prabhus tvam kila vaisnavi para 5.15.23

“Eternally Krsna is Lord Narayana. Eternally You are Goddess Laksmi. Eternally Krsna is Lord Brahma. Eternally You are Goddess Sarasvati. Eternally Krsna is Lord Siva. Eternally You are Goddess Parvati. Eternally Krsna is the auspicious Supreme Personality of Godhead. Eternally You are His greatest devotee.”

kaumara-sargi harir adi-devata tvam eva hi jnanamayi smrtih cubha layambhasa kridana-tat-paro harir yajno varaho vasudha tvam eva hi kaumara 5.15.24

“Krsna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the father of the four Kumaras. You are the auspicious Sruti-castra. Krsna is Yajna Varaha, who plays on the ocean of devastation. You are the earth goddess.”

devarsi-varyo manasa harih svayam tvam tatra saksan nija-hasta-vallaki narayano dharma-suto narena hi cantis tada tvam jana-canti-karini devarsi 5.15.25

“Krsna is Narada, the best of divine sages. You are the vina in His hand. Krsna is Narayana Rsi, the son of Dharma and the companion of Nara. You are Santi, who bring peace to the living entities.”

krsnas tu saksat kapilo mahaprabhuh siddhis tvam evasi ca siddha-sevita dattas tu krsno ’sti maha-municvaro radhe sada jnanamayi tvam eva hi 5.15.26

“Krsna is Lord Kapila. You are the mystic perfections served by the Siddhas. Krsna is Dattatreya, the king of the great sages. You are the perfection of transcendental knowledge eternally.”

yajno haris tvam kila daksina harir urukramas tvam hi sada jayanty atah prthur yada sarva-nrpecvaro harir arcis tada tvam nrpa-patta-kamini 5.15.27

“Krsna is Yajna. You are Daksina. Krsna is Urukrama. You are Jayanti eternally. Krsna is Prthu, the master of all kings. You are beautiful Queen Arci.”

cagkhasuram hantum abhud harir yada matsyavataras tvam asi crutis tada kurmo harir madara-sindhu-manthane netri-krta tvam cubhada hi vasuki 5.15.28

“When Krsna appeared as Matsyavatara to kill Sagkhasura, You became the Four Vedas. When, during the churning of the milk-ocean, Krsna appeared as Kurma, You became Vasuki, the auspicious churning-rope.”

dhanvantaric carti-haro harih paras tvam ausadhi divya-sudhamayi cubhe sri-krsnacandras tu babhuva mohini tvam mohini tatra jagad-vimohini 5.15.29

“O beautiful one, when Krsna became Dhanvantari, the physician who cures all sufferings, You became His medicine of transcendental nectar. When Krsna became Mohini, You also became Mohini, the beautiful girl that bewilders all the worlds.”

harir nrsimhas tu nrsimha-lilaya lila tada tvam nija-bhakta-vatsala babhuva krsnas tu yada hi vamanah kirtis tada tvam nija-loka-kirtita 5.15.30

“When Krsna became Nrsimha You became His man-lion pastimes, which were so kind to His devotee. When Krsna became Vamana, You became His fame, which is chanted by the devotees.”

harir yada bhargava-rupa-dhrk puman dhara kutharasya tada tvam eva hi sri-krsnacandro raghu-vamca-candrama yada tada tvam janakasya nandini 5.15.31

“When Krsna became Paracurama You became the sharpness of His ax. When Krsna became Rama, the moon of the Raghavas, You became Sita, the daughter of King Janaka.”

sri-cargga-dhanva muni-badarayano vedanta-krt tvam kila veda-laksana sagkarsano madhava eva vrsnisu tvam revati brahma-bhava samasthita sri-cargga 5.15.32

“When Krsna, who holds the Sargga bow, becomes the sage Vyasa and writes the Vedanta-sutra, You become His commentary on the Vedas. When Krsna becomes Balarama among the Vrsnis, You become Brahma-bhava Revati.”

buddho yada kaunapa-moha-karako buddhis tada tvam jana-moha-karini kalki yada dharma-patir bhavisyati haris tada tvam sukrtir bhavisyati 5.15.33

“When Krsna becomes Buddha and bewilders the atheists, You will become the intelligence by which He bewilders them. When Krsna becomes Kalki, the master of religion, You will become His pious deeds.”

sri-krsnacandro ’sti hi candra-mandale radhe sada candra-mukhiti candrika sri-krsna-suryo divi surya-mandale surya-prabha tvam paridhi-pratisthita sri 5.15.34

“O moon-faced Radha, Sri Krsna is a moon, and You are His moonlight eternally. Sri Krsna is a sun, and You are His sunlight eternally.”

indrah sadaste kila yadavendras tatraiva radhe tu caci cacicvari hiranya-reta hi harih parecvaro hetih sada tvam hi hiranmayi para 5.15.35

“Krsna, the king of the Yadavas, is Indra, and You are His saintly queen Saci eternally. Krsna is a fire, and You are eternally His splendid light.”

sri-raja-rajo hi virajate harir virajase tvam tu nidhau nidhicvari ksirabdhi-rupi tu haris tvam eva hi taraggita-ksauma-sita taraggini sri-raja 5.15.36

“Krsna is Kuvera, and You are the queen of His treasury. Krsna is the ocean of milk, and You are the silk-white waves on the ocean of milk.”

bibhrad vapuh sarva-patir yada yada tada tada tvam viditanurupini jagan-mayo brahma-mayo harih svayam jagan-mayi brahma-mayi tvam eva hi 5.15.37

“Whenever Lord Krsna, the master of all, assumes another form, You follow Him and assume the form of His companion. When He is the universal form or the impersonal Brahman, You also become the universal form or the impersonal Brahman.”

athaiva so ’yam vraja-raja-nandano jatasi radhe vrsabhanu-nandini yabhyam krta sattva-mayi pracantaye lila-caritrair lalitadi-lilaya 5.15.38

“Now He is the son of Vraja’s king, so now You are born as Vrsabhanu’s daughter. With Your charming pastimes You bring peace and happiness to Your devotees.”

krsnah svayam brahma param purano lila tad-iccha-prakrtis tvam eva parasparam sandhita-vigrahabhyam namo yuvabhyam hari-radhikabhyam 5.15.39

“Radha and Krsna. Krsna is the perfect Supreme Personality of Godhead, and You are His pastimes and the potency that fulfills His desires. I offer my respectful obeisances to You, Sri Sri Radha and Krsna, the Supreme, whose forms are eternal.”

grhana patram nija-natha-dattam cokam param ma kuru radhike tvam hrasvena kalena vidhaya karyam tatragamisyami tad-ukta-vakyam 5.15.40

“O Radha, please take this letter. Don’t lament. Krsna said, “Soon I will finish My duties here. Then I will return.”

Chapter 16 - Comforting Sri Radha and the Gopis

radha patram saggrhitva ciro netre tatha ca hrt nidhaya vacayitva tat smrtva tat-pada-pagkajam radhaRadha; 5.16.1

“Taking the letter, placing it over Her head, eyes, and heart, and remembering Krsna’s lotus feet, Radha read the letter aloud.”

ati-prematura rajan mocayitvacru-santatim murcham apa param radha yadavasya prapacyatah ati 5.16.2

“O king, as Uddhava watched, Radha, overcome with love, shed many tears, and then fainted unconscious.”

kugkumaguru-patira-dvaraih puspa-rasaic ca sa arcita camarandolaih punac caitanyatam gatah 5.16.3

“Anointed with kugkuma, aguru, sandal, and flower-nectar, and fanned with camaras, Radha regained consciousness.”

viyoga-sindhu-sammagnam radham kamala-locanam viksyoddhavas tatah gopyo mumucuc cacru-santatim 5.16.4

“Seeing lotus-eyed Radha drowning in an ocean of despair by Lord Krsna’s absence, Uddhava and the gopis shed many tears.”

tasam acru-pravahena rajan vrndavane vane sadyah kahlara-samyukto jato lila-sarovarah 5.16.5

“O king, from the stream of their tears Lake Lila-sarovara, filled with many lotus flowers, was created at that moment.”

drstva pitva ca su-snatva crutva cemam katham narah karma-bandha-vinirmuktah sri-krsnam prapnuyan nrpa 5.16.6

“O king, a person who sees, drinks from, or bathes in that lake, or who listens to this story, becoms free from the bonds of karma and attains Lord Krsna.”

athoddhava-mukhac chrutva sri-krsnagamanam punah papracchuh kucalam sarvam sri-krsnasya mahatmanah 5.16.7

“After hearing from Uddhava’s mouth that Lord Krsna would return, the gopis asked about Lord Krsna’s welfare.”

sri-radhovaca ananda-dam sri-vraja-raja-nandanam draksyami kasmin samaye ghana-prabham ghanam mayuriva samutsuka bhrcam candram cakoriva tad-iksanotsuka sri-radha 5.16.8

Sri Radha said: “When will I see Vraja’s delightful prince splendid as a monsoon cloud? I yearn to see Him as a peacock yearns to see a monsoon cloud or a cakori bird yearns to see the moon.”

kasmin ku-kale viraho babhuva me yenaiva kau kalpa-samah ksanah ksanah nicithiniyam dvi-parardha-helanam karoti govinda-pada-dvayam vina 5.16.9

“Since that painful time when I was separated from Him, every moment has become a kalpa. Without Krsna’s feet, every night mocks the span of Brahma’s life.”

kaccit kadacid vrajam agamisyati karoti kim tatra harir vadacu me adyaiva yatnena dhrtah kilasavah prasahya niryanti mrsa-giraturah 5.16.10

“Will Krsna return? What does He do there. Please tell Me. With a great struggle I have held onto My life-breath. If you torture it with many lies, it will quickly escape.”

drstva ksanam tvam mama hrc ca citalam jatam prasannasmi samagate tvayi yatha prasanna janakatmaja pura lagka-puram vayu-sute samagate 5.16.11

“When I first saw you My feverish heart became cool. Now that you have come, I am happy. I am happy as Sita was happy when Hanuman came to Lagka City.”

acam vidhaya nija-moha-dhanam visrjya visrjya vakya-gaditam mathuram gato yah tasyapi patra-likhitam hy amrtam na manye tam canayasva kila mantra-vidam varistha 5.16.12

“I have given up all hope. I have thrown away a host of illusions. Now that He has gone to Mathura, I do not believe His words. I do not think His letter has words of nectar. O Uddhava, O best of counselors, please bring Him here.”

sri-uddhava uvaca gatva purim tava param viraham nivedya-thargham vidhaya nija-netra-jalena radhe nitva harim tava purah punar agato ’smi ma cokam adya kuru me capathas tvad-agghreh sri-uddhava 5.16.13

Sri Uddhava said: “I will go to Mathura City, describe to Him how You feel in His absence, offer Him arghya with the tears from my eyes, and then, O Radha, I will bring Krsna to You. Please don’t lament. I place this promise at Your feet.”

sri-narada uvaca atha prasanna sri-radha candrakantau mani cubhau rasa-ragge candra-dattau uddhavaya dadau nrpa sri-narada 5.16.14

Sri Narada said: “O king, happy to hear this, Sri Radha gave to Uddhava two candrakanta jewels that moonlight had placed in the rasa-dance arena.”

sahasra-dala-padme dve datte candramasa pura uddhavaya dadau radha prasanna bhakta-vatsala sahasra-dala 5.16.15

“Then Radha, who is kind to the devotees, happily gave Uddhava two thousand-petal lotuses that had also been created by moonlight.”

chatram simhasanam divyam camare dve manohare sri-krsna-manasodbhute dadau tasmai hari-priya 5.16.16

“Then Krsna’s beloved Radha gave to Uddhava a parasol, a a splendid throne, and two beautiful camaras that had been created by Lord Krsna’s thoughts.”

aicvaryam jnana-sampannam sarva-decika-decikam krsna-samyoga-kartrtvam sada tava bhavisyati 5.16.17

“Then She said to Uddhava, “You will always have a great wealth of transcendental knowledge, knowledge that enables you to meet Lord Krsna.”

bhaktim nirguna-bhavadhyam prema-laksana-samyutam jnanam vijnana-sahitam vairagyam sa dadau punah 5.16.18

“The She gave him devotion beyond the modes of nature, pure love for Krsna, knowledge, wisdom, and renunciation.”

cagkhacuda ca harina-nitam cuda-manim cubham candranana dadau tasmai uddhavaya videha-rat 5.16.19

“O king of Videha, then moon-faced Radha gave to Uddhava the beautiful crest-jewel Lord Krsna took from Sagkhacuda.”

tatha gopi-ganah sarve bhusananam cayam cubham daduh prasanna he rajann uddhavaya mahatmane 5.16.20

“Then all the gopis happily gave many ornaments to noble-hearted Uddhava.”

sri-narada uvaca crutva vacac copagaveh cubhartham sukham gatayam kila radhikayam ucus tam arad vraja-gopa-vadhvah sadah-sthitam krsna-sakham prthak tah sri-narada 5.16.21

Sri Narada said: “When She heard Uddhava’s comforting words, Sri Radha became happy. Then the Vraja-gopis surrounded Krsna’s friend Uddhava and spoke.”

sri-gopya ucuh yac ca patra-likhitam vadacu nah kintu tac ca harinoktam adbhutam tvam paravara-vidam hareh sakha mantra-vittama tadakrtir mahan sri-gopya 5.16.22

The gopis said: “O best of counselors, You are a very important person. You know everything. Your form is like Krsna’s and You are Krsna’s personal friend. Please read Krsna’s wonderful letter to us.”

sri-uddhava uvaca yatha smaratha devecam tatha yusman smaraty asau anuvelam gopa-vadhvah pacyato me na samcayah sri-uddhava 5.16.23

Sri Uddhava said: “As you remember Lord Krsna, so He always remembers you. O gopis, I have seen this myself. There is no doubt of this.”

ekada mam samahuya smrtva yusman rahas-karah kathayam asa sandecam citta-stham nanda-nandanah 5.16.24

“One day Lord Krsna called me to His private room and from His heart dicatated the following letter:.”

sri-bhagavan uvaca ganesu saktam kila bandhanaya raktam manah pumsi ca muktaye syat mano dvayoh karanam ahur araj jitvatha tat kau vicared asaggah sri-bhagavan 5.16.25

The Blessed Lord said: “Attraction to the modes of material nature lead to bondage, but attraction to the Supreme Personality of Godhead leads to liberation. Thus the mind may be the cause of bondage or the cause of liberation. The wise therefore control the mind and remain unattached to the world of matter.”

yada svayam brahma parat param mam adhyatma-yogena vicaradena janati sarvatra gatam viveki tada vijahyan manasah kasayam yavad ghano madhya-gatas tad-utthitah sva-karma-rupam na hi drk prapacyati 5.16.26

“By expertly practicing yoga, a wise person sees Me, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, everywhere, and when he sees Me, his mind is no longer attracted to material things. As one cannot see clouds chased away by the rising sun, so he cannot see the results of his past fruitive acts.”

Chapter 17 - The Gopis Describe Their Remembrance of Sri Krsna

sri-narada uvaca crutva sri-krsna-sandecam prasanna gopa-vallabhah acru-mukhyo baspa-kanthyam ucur aupagavim nrpa sri-narada 5.17.1

Sri Narada said: “O king, happy to hear Sri Krsna’s letter, and tears streaming down their faces and necks, the gopis spoke.”

sri-goloka-vasinya ucuh videcam gatavan krsnas tykatva purva-priyan janan tad upary alikhad yogam aho nirmohata-balam sri-goloka-vasinya 5.17.2

The gopis from Sri Goloka said: “Krsna left the people He used to love and went far away. Now He writes us a letter saying yoga makes one free from illusions.”

sri-dvara-palika ucuh cakore glauh pagkaje ’rko bhramare pagkajam yatha catake ca ghanah pritim na karoti kadacana sri-dvara-palika 5.17.3
